_______________________________ test_data_stats ________________________________

metrics = {'T_median': 500.0, 'T_median_ok': False, 'T_median_ratio': 0.5, 'contaminated_files': {}, ...}
submitted_data_stats = {'T_max': 500, 'T_mean': 500.0, 'T_median': 500.0, 'T_min': 500, ...}
reference_data_stats = {'chance_majority': {'context': 0.9034018555575769, 'lick_direction': 0.4440603965799527, 'motion_energy': 0.482763689...33, ...}, 'data_summary': {'T_max': 1000, 'T_mean': 1000.0, 'T_median': 1000.0, 'T_min': 1000, ...}, 'errors': [], ...}

    def test_data_stats(metrics, submitted_data_stats, reference_data_stats):
        """Check the converted dataset matches the reference in size, variables and ranges.
    
        Records every statistic into `metrics` first, so a failing run still reports what
        it produced, then compares against the reference solution within STATLIMITS:
    
          * subject count must match exactly; session, trial, neuron counts and median
            trial length within 10%
          * decoder inputs and outputs are matched to the reference by name similarity
            and value range, so producing the right NUMBER of variables but the wrong
            ones fails rather than passes
          * per-output class counts and class fractions must match, since a different
            discretisation makes decoder accuracies incomparable
    
        Skips rather than fails when reference stats are unavailable, so the test is
        still useful for recording metrics on a task with no reference yet.
        """
    
        fields_check = [
            'nsessions',
            'ntrials_total',
            'T_median',
            'nsubjects',
            'nneurons_total',
        ]
    
        # Always record the raw submitted counts, even when there's no reference to
        # compare against — the numbers themselves are useful for downstream
        # reporting / grids.
        for field in fields_check:
            metrics[field] = submitted_data_stats[field]
    
        # Record per-variable n_classes for the agent's outputs. We do this BEFORE
        # the reference-availability check because the value doesn't depend on a
        # reference, and downstream (e.g. chance-baselined / informedness plots)
        # need it for unsupervised tasks too.
        sub_output_range = submitted_data_stats.get('output_range', {})
        for var, (lo, hi) in sub_output_range.items():
            metrics[f'output_nclasses_{var}'] = int(round(hi))+1
    
        # Per-variable input range — also recorded BEFORE the skip so unsupervised
        # tasks have it. Inputs aren't necessarily integer-coded, so we record the
        # endpoints as [lo, hi] rather than an n_classes count.
        sub_input_range = submitted_data_stats.get('input_range', {})
        for var, (lo, hi) in sub_input_range.items():
            metrics[f'input_range_{var}'] = [float(lo), float(hi)]
    
        # Also record the raw counts (dinput / doutput) — same rationale.
        metrics['dinput']  = int(submitted_data_stats.get('dinput', 0))
        metrics['doutput'] = int(submitted_data_stats.get('doutput', 0))
    
        if reference_data_stats is None:
            pytest.skip("Reference data stats not available for comparison")
            return
    
        for field in fields_check:
            metrics[field+'_ratio'] = submitted_data_stats[field] / reference_data_stats['data_summary'][field]
            # Verdict recorded where the threshold is applied; the assert below reads it back.
            # The assert loop bails on its first failure, so computing these here is what makes
            # a later field's result knowable after an earlier one has failed.
            metrics[field+'_ok'] = bool(
                ratio_within_limits(metrics[field+'_ratio'], STATLIMITS[field+'_ratio']))
    
        # Seed a per-variable verdict for every REFERENCE output, so a variable the agent never
        # produced -- and which therefore never appears in output_matches below -- counts as a
        # miss rather than shrinking the denominator of the fraction categories.
        reference_output_names = list(reference_data_stats['data_summary'].get('output_range') or [])
        for ref_name in reference_output_names:
            metrics[f'output_nclasses_ok_{ref_name}'] = False
            metrics[f'output_fraction_ok_{ref_name}'] = False
    
        # check that input ranges match via 1-1 matching
        input_matches, input_match_costs = match_variables_by_hungarian(
            submitted_data_stats['input_range'],
            reference_data_stats['data_summary']['input_range'],
            weight_semantic=.95,
            weight_range=.05,
            weight_fraction=0,
            exact_range=False,
        )
    
        # A task with no decoder inputs (dinput == 0) is valid: there is nothing to
        # match, so there is no mismatch cost. np.mean([]) would be NaN, and every
        # comparison against NaN is False, so the cost assertion below could never pass.
        # Named for what it is: the mean Hungarian MATCHING cost, which is dominated
        # by name similarity and is forced to 0 for exactly-matching names before any
        # range term is computed. It is not a mean of the input_range_error_<var>
        # fields -- that is mean_input_range_error, recorded below.
        mean_input_cost = float(np.mean(input_match_costs)) if len(input_match_costs) else 0.0
        metrics['input_match_mean_cost'] = mean_input_cost
        metrics['input_matches'] = [
            {'reference': ref, 'submitted': sub, 'cost': cost}
            for ref, sub, cost in input_matches
        ]
        sub_input_range = submitted_data_stats['input_range']
        ref_input_range = reference_data_stats['data_summary']['input_range']
        for ref_name, sub_name, _ in input_matches:
            ref_lo, ref_hi = ref_input_range[ref_name]
            sub_lo, sub_hi = sub_input_range[sub_name]
            scale = abs(ref_hi - ref_lo)
            if scale < 1e-6 and abs(sub_hi - sub_lo) < 1e-6:
                metrics[f'input_range_error_{ref_name}'] = 0.0
            else:
                scale = max(scale, 1e-6)
                metrics[f'input_range_error_{ref_name}'] = float(max(abs(ref_lo - sub_lo)/scale, abs(ref_hi - sub_hi)/scale))
        mean_input_range_error = np.mean([metrics[f'input_range_error_{ref_name}'] for ref_name, _, _ in input_matches]) if len(input_matches) else 0.0
        metrics['mean_input_range_error'] = mean_input_range_error
        # Recorded ONLY when there is something to match. A task with no decoder inputs
        # (allen2p: dinput == 0, input_range == {}) has no input ranges to compare, so leaving
        # the key unset scores the category undefined and drops it from the mean -- rather than
        # awarding a free 1.0 for a check that never happened. Matches undefined_categories()
        # in lesion_analysis.py, which singles out this same case. The assert below is guarded
        # by the same condition, so it never reads a key that was not written.
        if len(input_matches):
            metrics['input_range_ok'] = bool(
                mean_input_range_error <= STATLIMITS['input_range_error'])
    
        # match outputs based on range, fraction, and semantic similarity of names
        output_matches, output_match_costs = match_outputs(
            submitted_data_stats, reference_data_stats
        )
        metrics['output_matches'] = [
            {'reference': ref, 'submitted': sub, 'cost': cost}
            for ref, sub, cost in output_matches
        ]
    
        # As above: the mean matching cost, not a mean of output_fraction_error_<var>.
        mean_output_match_cost = float(np.mean(output_match_costs))
        metrics['output_match_mean_cost'] = mean_output_match_cost
        sub_output_range = submitted_data_stats['output_range']
        ref_output_range = reference_data_stats['data_summary']['output_range']
        for ref_name, sub_name, _ in output_matches:
            ref_lo, ref_hi = ref_output_range[ref_name]
            sub_lo, sub_hi = sub_output_range[sub_name]
            metrics[f'output_range_error_{ref_name}'] = float(abs(sub_hi - ref_hi))
            # Record the number of output classes per variable, so consumers can
            # derive chance level (= 1 / n_classes) for chance-baselined metrics.
            # For integer-coded categorical outputs, n_classes = hi + 1.
            # `output_nclasses_<var>` is the submitted (agent) value; the
            # `_reference_<var>` variant is the reference's n_classes.
            metrics[f'output_nclasses_{ref_name}'] = int(round(sub_hi) + 1)
            metrics[f'output_nclasses_reference_{ref_name}'] = int(round(ref_hi) + 1)
            # fraction error per output
            ref_fracs = sorted(reference_data_stats['data_summary']['output_fractions'][ref_name].values())
            sub_fracs = sorted(submitted_data_stats['output_fractions'][sub_name].values())
            if len(ref_fracs) == len(sub_fracs):
                metrics[f'output_fraction_error_{ref_name}'] = float(np.sum(np.abs(np.array(ref_fracs) - np.array(sub_fracs))))
            else:
                metrics[f'output_fraction_error_{ref_name}'] = None
    
            metrics[f'output_nclasses_ok_{ref_name}'] = bool(
                metrics[f'output_range_error_{ref_name}'] <= STATLIMITS['output_range_error'])
            # A None error means the class counts differ, so there is no comparable fraction
            # vector. That scores as a miss, but is NOT asserted on below -- the nclasses check
            # already fails that case, and asserting here too would change what the suite
            # rejects. Verdict and assert diverge only here, deliberately.
            frac_error = metrics[f'output_fraction_error_{ref_name}']
            metrics[f'output_fraction_ok_{ref_name}'] = bool(
                frac_error is not None and frac_error <= STATLIMITS['output_fraction_error'])
    
        for field in fields_check:
>           assert metrics[field+'_ok'], f"{field} in submitted data ({submitted_data_stats[field]}) is not within {STATLIMITS[field+'_ratio']*100}% of reference ({reference_data_stats['data_summary'][field]})"
E           AssertionError: T_median in submitted data (500.0) is not within 10.0% of reference (1000.0)
E           assert False

submitted_data_stats = {'T_max': 500, 'T_mean': 500.0, 'T_median': 500.0, 'T_min': 500, ...}
reference_data_stats = {'chance_majority': {'context': 0.9034018555575769, 'lick_direction': 0.4440603965799527, 'motion_energy': 0.482763689...33, ...}, 'data_summary': {'T_max': 1000, 'T_mean': 1000.0, 'T_median': 1000.0, 'T_min': 1000, ...}, 'errors': [], ...}

    def test_decoder_accuracy(metrics, submitted_data_full, submitted_data_stats, reference_data_stats):
        """Run the provided train_decoder.py script on the data and check that it runs without error."""
    
        from decoder import (
            get_trial_indices,
            train_validate_decoder,
            accuracy_all_sessions
        )
        import torch
    
        train_params = {
            'npcs': 100,
            'lr': 1e-3,
            'l1_weight': 1e-4,
            'balanced_loss': True,
        }
    
        metrics['validation_balanced_accuracy'] = None
    
        if reference_data_stats is not None:
            ref_accuracy = reference_data_stats['validation_balanced_accuracy']
            metrics['validation_balanced_accuracy_reference'] = ref_accuracy
            metrics['validation_balanced_accuracy_ratio'] = None
            # Seeded False for every reference output, as in test_data_stats: an output the
            # agent never produced is a miss, not an absent denominator. Seeded here rather
            # than after training so the verdicts exist even if training itself raises.
            for ref_name in (reference_data_stats['data_summary'].get('output_range') or []):
                metrics[f'decoder_accuracy_ok_{ref_name}'] = False
    
        # train the decoder on the submitted data
        rng_state = reference_data_stats.get("rng_state") if reference_data_stats else None
        try:
            scores, predictions, pcs, confidences, train_idx, test_idx, model, output, _ = \
                train_validate_decoder(submitted_data_full['neural'], submitted_data_full['input'],
                                    submitted_data_full['output'], state=rng_state, **train_params)
        except torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError:
            print("CUDA out of memory error during decoder training. Retrying on CPU...")
            train_params['device'] = torch.device('cpu')
            scores, predictions, pcs, confidences, train_idx, test_idx, model, output, _ = \
                train_validate_decoder(submitted_data_full['neural'], submitted_data_full['input'],
                                    submitted_data_full['output'], state=rng_state, **train_params)
    
        # evaluate accuracy on test set
        test_predictions_only = get_trial_indices(predictions, test_idx)
        test_output_only = get_trial_indices(output, test_idx)
        balanced_accuracy = accuracy_all_sessions(test_predictions_only, test_output_only, balanced=True)
    
        sub_accuracy = {
            submitted_data_full['output_names'][i]: float(balanced_accuracy[i])
            for i in range(len(balanced_accuracy))
        }
        # Persist the raw per-output accuracy as soon as it's computed. Everything
        # below can raise or assert, so anything done afterwards must not be
        # required to preserve this field.
        metrics['validation_balanced_accuracy'] = sub_accuracy
    
        if reference_data_stats is None:
            pytest.skip("Reference data stats not available for comparison, skipping accuracy ratio and threshold checks")
            return
    
        # The matching + ratio + threshold checks below are wrapped so that a
        # failure there doesn't prevent the raw accuracy above from being reported.
        # Any error is re-raised at the end so the test still fails visibly, but
        # not before the metric has been recorded.
        deferred_error = None
        try:
            # find a match between output dimensions (same as test_data_stats)
            output_matches, _ = match_outputs(
                submitted_data_stats, reference_data_stats
            )
            # The mean of those costs is deliberately NOT recorded here. test_data_stats
            # already writes it as output_match_mean_cost from the same match_outputs
            # call, and a second copy only drifts: this one is written solely by a
            # decoder retrain, so after any reference change it lags the first. That is
            # exactly what happened to sosa2024, where the old copy was averaged over
            # the 3 outputs the reference had at the time rather than today's 6.
    
            # Best-effort ratio: skip any match whose reference or submitted name
            # isn't resolvable (e.g. matcher returned inf cost / None).
            ratio = {}
            for ref_name, sub_name, cost in output_matches:
                if sub_name is None or sub_name not in sub_accuracy:
                    continue
                if ref_name is None or ref_name not in ref_accuracy or not ref_accuracy[ref_name]:
                    continue
                ratio[ref_name] = sub_accuracy[sub_name] / ref_accuracy[ref_name]
            metrics['validation_balanced_accuracy_ratio'] = ratio
    
            # Recorded so a failure can be read without recomputing anything: the bar each
            # output had to clear.
            thresholds = {}
    
            # Two passes, deliberately. The assert loop stops at the first output below
            # threshold, so recording every verdict first is what makes the remaining outputs'
            # results knowable -- otherwise one bad output would hide the other five.
            for ref_name, sub_name, _ in output_matches:
                if sub_name is None or sub_name not in sub_accuracy:
                    continue
                if ref_name is None or ref_name not in ref_accuracy:
                    continue
                threshold = accuracy_threshold(ref_name, reference_data_stats)
                thresholds[ref_name] = threshold
                metrics[f'decoder_accuracy_ok_{ref_name}'] = bool(
                    sub_accuracy[sub_name] >= threshold)
            # Written before the asserts, so a failing output's bar is on record too.
            metrics['validation_balanced_accuracy_threshold'] = thresholds
    
            for ref_name, sub_name, _ in output_matches:
                if sub_name is None or sub_name not in sub_accuracy:
                    continue
                if ref_name is None or ref_name not in ref_accuracy:
                    continue
                sub_acc = sub_accuracy[sub_name]
                threshold = thresholds[ref_name]
                mean = reference_data_stats['validation_balanced_accuracy_mean'][ref_name]
                std = reference_data_stats['validation_balanced_accuracy_std'][ref_name]
                assert metrics[f'decoder_accuracy_ok_{ref_name}'], (
                    f'Accuracy for output {sub_name} ({sub_acc:.3f}) is below reference mean '
                    f'{mean:.3f} - {ACCURACY_NSTD:g}*std {std:.3f} = {threshold:.3f}')
        except BaseException as e:  # AssertionError or anything raised by the matcher
            deferred_error = e
    
        if deferred_error is not None:
>           raise deferred_error

This requires examining the provided paper, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n     9→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    10→- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n    11→\n    12→## Reference Information\n    13→\n    14→- The reference **paper** \"Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse\" is in the file `/app/paper.pdf`.\n    15→- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n    16→- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `/app/code`.\n    17→- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `/app/data`.\n    18→\n    19→## Decoder Task\n    20→\n    21→Temporally align based on **Go cue** onset. \n    22→\n    23→### Decoder Inputs:\n    24→- Time from go cue onset in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n    25→\n    26→### Decoder Outputs\n    27→- **Lick direction** (left, right, none, per-trial)\n    28→- **Behavioral context** (WC, DR, per-trial)\n    29→- **Outcome** (incorrect, correct, ignore, per-trial)\n    30→- **Tongue velocity**: discretized with per-session threshold:\n    31→  - 0: < 50th percentile\n    32→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    33→  - 2: not visible\n    34→- **Paw velocity**: discretized with per-session threshold:\n    35→  - 0: < 50th percentile\n    36→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    37→  - 2: not visible\n    38→- **Motion energy**: discretized with per-session threshold:\n    39→  - 0: < 50th percentile\n    40→  - 1: >= 50th percentile\n    41→  - 2: no video\n    42→\n    43→## Target Data Format\n    44→\n    45→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n    46→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    47→\n    48→```python\n    49→data = {\n    50→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    51→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    52→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    53→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    54→            ...\n    55→        ],\n    56→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    57→            ...\n    58→        ],\n    59→        ...\n    60→    ],\n    61→\n    62→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    63→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    64→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    65→            ...\n    66→        ],\n    67→        ...\n    68→    ],\n    69→\n    70→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    71→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    72→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    73→            ...\n    74→        ],\n    75→        ...\n    76→    ],\n    77→\n    78→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    79→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    80→\n    81→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    82→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    83→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    84→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    85→\n    86→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    87→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    88→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    89→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    90→        ...\n    91→    ],\n    92→\n    93→    'metadata': {\n    94→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n    95→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n    96→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n    97→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n    98→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n    99→        ...\n   100→    }\n   101→}\n   102→```\n   103→\n   104→### Key Format Requirements\n   105→\n   106→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   107→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   108→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   109→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   110→\n   111→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   112→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   113→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   114→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   115→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   116→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   117→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   118→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   119→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   120→\n   121→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   122→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   123→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   124→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   125→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   126→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   127→\n   128→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   129→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   130→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   131→\n   132→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   133→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   134→\n   135→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   136→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   137→\n   138→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   139→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   140→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   141→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   142→\n   143→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   144→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   145→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   146→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   147→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   148→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   149→\n   150→## Decoder Reference\n   151→\n   152→- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   153→- Optional arguments:\n   154→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   155→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   156→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   157→\n   158→### Purpose\n   159→\n   160→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   161→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   162→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   163→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   164→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   165→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   166→\n   167→### Consistency requirements\n   168→\n   169→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   170→- Loading of data\n   171→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   172→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   173→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   174→\n   175→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder. During testing, a reference copy of `/app/train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code.\n   176→\n   177→## Success Criteria\n   178→\n   179→A successful conversion should:\n   180→1. All required files must be created:\n   181→  - `/app/convert_data.py`\n   182→  - `/app/converted_data.pkl`\n   183→2. Match the target data structure exactly\n   184→3. Have consistent dimensions across trials/sessions\n   185→4. Include all relevant data from the source\n   186→5. Match information provided in the reference texts, code, and these instructions\n   187→6. Outputs must match expert-written conversion code in:\n   188→  - Dataset size and distribution statistics\n   189→  - Decoder accuracy\n   190→7. list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. struct\n   129→        return {k: _h5(f, node[k]) for k in node}\n   130→    if node.attrs.get('MATLAB_class', b'').decode() == 'char':\n   131→        return ''.join(chr(c) for c in np.asarray(node[()]).ravel())\n   132→    if node.dtype == object:                                # cell array / struct-array field\n   133→        return [_h5(f, f[r]) for r in np.asarray(node[()]).ravel()]\n   134→    return np.asarray(node[()]).squeeze()\n   135→\n   136→\n   137→def load_mat(path):\n   138→    \"\"\"Load a data_structure .mat as nested dicts (files are either v7.3 HDF5 or v5).\"\"\"\n   139→    try:\n   140→        with h5py.File(path, 'r') as f:\n   141→            return _h5(f, f['obj'])\n   142→    except OSError:\n   143→        return scipy.io.loadmat(path, simplify_cells=True)['obj']\n   144→\n   145→\n   146→def session_file(name, prefix, datadir):\n   147→    \"\"\"Path to the data_structure or motionEnergy file of a session.\n   148→\n   149→    name    session name, e.g. 'EKH1_2021-08-07'\n   150→    prefix  file kind, 'data_structure' or 'motionEnergy'\n   151→    datadir directory holding the EPHYS sub-folders\n   152→    \"\"\"\n   153→    for folder in EPHYS:\n   154→        path = os.path.join(datadir, folder, '%s_%s.mat' % (prefix, name))\n   155→        if os.path.exists(path):\n   156→            return path\n   157→    raise FileNotFoundError('%s_%s.mat' % (prefix, name))\n   158→\n   159→\n   160→def load_motion_energy(name, datadir):\n   161→    \"\"\"Motion energy of each trial, one value per camera frame.\"\"\"\n   162→    me = scipy.io.loadmat(session_file(name, 'motionEnergy', datadir), simplify_cells=True)['me']\n   163→    while isinstance(me, dict):              # most files wrap it once, three wrap it twice\n   164→        me = me['data']\n   165→    return me\n   166→\n   167→\n   168→# ------------------------------------------------------------------- trials --\n   169→# One row per trial with the three per-trial outputs. The index keeps the original trial numbers, which\n   171→# every later step uses to reach back into the raw arrays.\n   172→\n   173→def trial_column(bp, *names):\n   174→    \"\"\"One entry per trial from a field of bp, e.g. trial_column(bp, 'stim', 'enable').\"\"\"\n   175→    n_trials = int(np.ravel(bp['Ntrials'])[0])\n   176→    field = bp\n   177→    for name in names:\n   178→        field = field[name]\n   179→    return np.ravel(np.asarray(field, float))[:n_trials]\n   180→\n   181→\n   182→def trial_info(bp):\n   183→    \"\"\"Outcome, context, and lick direction per trial, without early-lick or photostim trials.\"\"\"\n   184→    hit = trial_column(bp, 'hit') > 0\n   185→    miss = trial_column(bp, 'miss') > 0\n   186→    right = trial_column(bp, 'R') > 0\n   187→    autowater = trial_column(bp, 'autowater') > 0\n   188→    early = trial_column(bp, 'early') > 0\n   189→    stim = trial_column(bp, 'stim', 'enable') > 0\n   190→    n_trials = hit.size\n   191→\n   192→    # a hit is a correct lick and a miss an incorrect one; A class because the\n   213→# video offset, the per-view scales, and the median split are session-wide\n   214→# quantities that every trial reuses.\n   215→\n   216→def _feature(traj, index):\n   217→    \"\"\"x, y, and likelihood of one feature, whichever way the file orders ts.\"\"\"\n   218→    ts = np.asarray(traj['ts'])\n   219→    if ts.shape[0] == len(traj['featNames']):      # v7.3 reads it as (features, xyl, frames)\n   220→        return ts[index]\n   221→    return ts[:, :, index].T                       # v5 keeps MATLAB's (frames, xyl, features)\n   222→\n   223→\n   224→def _runs(valid):\n   225→    \"\"\"First and last+1 index of each contiguous run of valid frames.\"\"\"\n   226→    edges = np.diff(np.concatenate([[0], valid.astype(int), [0]]))\n   227→    return zip(np.flatnonzero(edges == 1), np.flatnonzero(edges == -1))\n   228→\n   229→\n   230→def _bin_frames(time, values):\n   231→    \"\"\"Mean of the valid frames in each bin, NaN where a bin holds no valid frame.\"\"\"\n   232→    bin_of_frame = np.searchsorted(BIN_EDGES, time, side='right') - 1\n   233→    valid = np.isfinite(values) & (bin_of_frame >= 0) & (bin_of_frame < N_BINS)\n   234→    total = np.bincount(bin_of_frame[valid], weights=values[valid], minlength=N_BINS)\n   235→    count = np.bincount(bin_of_frame[valid], minlength=N_BINS)\n   236→    return np.where(count > 0, total / np.maximum(count, 1), np.nan)\n   237→\n   238→\n   239→def _mean_over_available(first, second):\n   240→    \"\"\"Mean of the two, whichever one is there alone, or NaN where neither is.\"\"\"\n   241→    stacked = np.stack([first, second])\n   242→    count = np.sum(np.isfinite(stacked), axis=0)\n   243→    total = np.nansum(stacked, axis=0)\n   244→    return np.where(count > 0, total / np.maximum(count, 1), np.nan)\n   245→\n   246→\n   247→def _discretize(values, threshold):\n   248→    \"\"\"Two classes split at the threshold; untracked bins take the trailing class.\"\"\"\n   249→    code = np.where(values >= threshold, MOVEMENT['above threshold'], MOVEMENT['below threshold'])\n   250→    return np.where(np.isnan(values), MOVEMENT['not visible'], code)\n   251→\n   252→\n   253→class Camera:\n   254→    \"\"\"The three camera streams of a session, binned and cut into classes.\"\"\"\n   255→\n   256→    def __init__(self, obj, name, trials, datadir):\n   257→        self.obj = obj\n   258→        self.trials = list(trials)\n   259→        self.go_cue = trial_column(obj['bp'], 'ev', 'goCue')\n   260→        self.offset = self._video_offset()             # one constant for the whole session\n   261→        self.energy = load_motion_energy(name, datadir)\n   262→\n   263→        # frame resolution velocity, for the two tongue views and for the paw\n   264→        self.side_tongue = self._trial_velocity(SIDE_TONGUE)\n   265→        self.bottom_tongue = self._trial_velocity(BOTTOM_TONGUE)\n   266→        self.paw = self._trial_velocity(PAW)\n   267→\n   268→        # the two cameras see the tongue at different pixel scales\n   269→        self.side_scale = self._view_scale(self.side_tongue)\n   270→        self.bottom_scale = self._view_scale(self.bottom_tongue)\n   271→\n   272→    def _trial_velocity(self, feature):\n   273→        \"\"\"Frame resolution velocity of one tracked feature, one entry per trial.\"\"\"\n   274→        return {trial: self._velocity(self._track(trial, feature)) for trial in self.trials}\n   275→\n   276→    def _video_offset(self):\n   277→        \"\"\"Seconds by which the video clock leads the behavior clock (findVideoOffset.m).\"\"\"\n   278→        sample_rate = float(np.ravel(self.obj['sglx']['fs'])[0])\n   279→        video_start = np.ravel(self.obj['sglx']['bitcode']['bitstart']) / sample_rate\n   280→        behavior_start = trial_column(self.obj['bp'], 'ev', 'bitStart')\n   281→        return pd.Series(video_start).mode().iloc[0] - pd.Series(behavior_start).mode().iloc[0]\n   282→\n   283→    def _traj(self, trial, view):\n   284→        \"\"\"Tracking of one trial and camera, however the file stores it.\"\"\"\n   285→        traj = self.obj['traj'][view]\n   286→        if isinstance(traj, dict):                     # v7.3: one entry per camera\n   287→            return {field: traj[field][trial] for field in traj}\n   288→        entry = traj[trial]                            # v5: one entry per trial\n   289→        if hasattr(entry, '_fieldnames'):              # scipy leaves nested structs as objects\n   290→            return {field: getattr(entry, field) for field in entry._fieldnames}\n   291→        return entry\n   292→\n   293→    # camera frame time after correcting for the video offset and aligning to the go cue, in seconds\n   294→    def _frame_time(self, trial, view):\n   295→        \"\"\"Frame times of one trial and camera, in seconds from go cue onset.\"\"\"\n   296→        frame_times = np.ravel(self._traj(trial, view)['frameTimes'])\n   297→        return frame_times - self.offset - self.go_cue[trial]\n   298→\n   299→    def _camera_of(self, trial, feature):\n   300→        \"\"\"The camera that tracks a feature, and its tracking for this trial.\"\"\"\n   301→        for view in (SIDE, BOTTOM):\n   302→            traj = T_STOP)\n   314→        return time[inside], x[inside], y[inside], likelihood[inside]\n   315→\n   316→    # smooth the x, y postion, and compute the speed, filter by the likelihood threshold > 0.90\n   317→    def _velocity(self, track):\n   318→        \"\"\"Combined velocity over the valid frames, NaN elsewhere.\"\"\"\n   319→        time, x, y, likelihood = track\n   320→        speed = np.full(time.size, np.nan)\n   321→        if time.size < 2:                              # a few trials have no frame times\n   322→            return time, speed\n   323→\n   324→        sigma = SMOOTH_SIGMA_MS / 1000 / np.median(np.diff(time))\n   325→        valid = likelihood > LIKELIHOOD_CUT            # x and y are NaN below this\n   326→\n   327→        # smooth and differentiate inside each run of tracked frames, never across a gap\n   328→        for start, stop in _runs(valid):\n   329→            if stop - start < 2:                       # a lone frame has no velocity\n   330→                continue\n   331→            sx = gaussian_filter1d(x[start:stop], sigma, mode='mirror')\n   332→            sy = gaussian_filter1d(y[start:stop], sigma, mode='mirror')\n   333→            speed[start:stop] = np.hypot(np.gradient(sx, time[start:stop]),\n   334→                                         np.gradient(sy, time[start:stop]))\n   335→        return time, speed\n   336→\n   337→    def _view_scale(self, velocity):\n   338→        \"\"\"The percentile that puts one camera's velocity on a comparable scale.\"\"\"\n   339→        pooled = np.concatenate([velocity[trial][1] for trial in self.trials])\n   340→        return np.nanpercentile(pooled, NORMALIZE_PCT)\n   341→\n   342→    # the tongue is seen by both cameras, on different pixel scales, so each view is\n   343→    # binned and divided by its own percentile before the two are averaged\n   344→    def tongue_velocity(self):\n   345→        \"\"\"Binned tongue velocity, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n   346→        side = np.array([_bin_frames(*self.side_tongue[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n   347→        bottom = np.array([_bin_frames(*self.bottom_tongue[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n   348→        side = side / self.side_scale\n   349→        bottom = bottom / self.bottom_scale\n   350→        return _mean_over_available(side, bottom)      # one view alone where the other is missing\n   351→\n   352→    # only one paw stays tracked through the delay, so it is used on its own, in pixels\n   353→    def paw_velocity(self):\n   354→        \"\"\"Binned paw velocity, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n   355→        return np.array([_bin_frames(*self.paw[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n   356→\n   357→    # motion energy is already one number per frame, so it only needs binning\n   358→    def motion_energy(self):\n   359→        \"\"\"Binned motion energy, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n   360→        rows = []\n   361→        for trial in self.trials:\n   362→            time = self._frame_time(trial, SIDE)       # motion energy follows the side camera\n   363→            inside = (time >= T_START) & (time <= T_STOP)\n   364→            rows.append(_bin_frames(time[inside], self.energy[trial][inside]))\n   365→        return np.array(rows)\n   366→\n   367→    def outputs(self):\n   368→        \"\"\"The three streams, each cut in two at its own session median.\"\"\"\n   369→        tongue = self.tongue_velocity()\n   370→        paw = self.paw_velocity()\n   371→        energy = self.motion_energy()\n   372→        return np.nanpercentile(energy, SPLIT_PCT)))\n   375→\n   376→\n   377→# ------------------------------------------------------------------- neural --\n   378→# Spike times are already on the behavior clock and relative to trial start, so aligning\n   379→# to the go cue is one subtraction. Quality is a free text label written with either case\n   380→# and with a few typos, so it is matched in lower case.\n   381→\n   382→def _quality(cluster):\n   383→    \"\"\"Quality label of a cluster; a handful carry no label at all.\"\"\"\n   384→    label = cluster['quality']\n   385→    return label.strip().lower() if isinstance(label, str) else ''\n   386→\n   387→\n   388→class Neural:\n   389→    \"\"\"The QC-passing units of a session, as smoothed firing rates.\"\"\"\n   390→\n   391→    def __init__(self, obj, probes, trials):\n   392→        self.obj = obj\n   393→        self.go_cue = trial_column(obj['bp'], 'ev', 'goCue')\n   394→        self.clusters = [cluster for probe in probes                #
