Codes follow the prompt's WC 0, DR 1.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Two per-trial flags of `obj.bp`: `hit` and `miss`. `bp.no` marks the ignore trials but is not read, since a trial that is neither a hit nor a miss is an ignore by construction — the three flags are mutually exclusive and sum to one on every trial of every session.\n\nii.\n```python\nhit = trial_column(bp, 'hit') > 0\nmiss = trial_column(bp, 'miss') > 0\n```\n\niii. The same two flags already determine lick direction, so outcome costs nothing extra to derive.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *outcome*?\n\ni. A relabelling into three classes: incorrect (0) on miss trials, correct (1) on hits, and ignore (2) where the animal did not respond.\n\nii.\n```python\nOUTCOME = {'incorrect': 0, 'correct': 1, 'ignore': 2}\n...\n# a hit is a correct lick and a miss an incorrect one; the animal ignored the rest\noutcome = np.full(n_trials, OUTCOME['ignore'])\noutcome[hit] = OUTCOME['correct']\noutcome[miss] = OUTCOME['incorrect']\n```\n\niii. The prompt specifies incorrect 0 and correct 1. The paper omits ignore trials from its analyses; here they are kept as a third class rather than dropped, so the trials stay in the dataset for the other five outputs.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *tongue_velocity* derived from?\n\ni. The DeepLabCut tracking in `obj.traj`, which holds one entry per camera. Each entry gives `featNames`, `frameTimes`, and `ts` — the tracked x, y, and likelihood of every feature on every frame. The tongue appears in both cameras, under `tongue` on the side view and `top_tongue` on the bottom view, and both are used. `bp.ev.goCue` and the bitcode fields in `obj.sglx` are also needed, to put the frames on the go-cue clock.\n\nii.\n```python\nSIDE_TONGUE = 'tongue'           # what the side camera calls the tongue\nBOTTOM_TONGUE = 'top_tongue'     # and what the bottom camera calls it\n...\nself.side_tongue = self._trial_velocity(SIDE_TONGUE)\nself.bottom_tongue = self._trial_velocity(BOTTOM_TONGUE)\n```\n\nWhere `featNames`, `ts`, and `frameTimes` are read:\n```python\ndef _track(self, trial, feature):\n    \"\"\"x, y, and likelihood of one feature over the frames inside the window.\"\"\"\n    view, traj = self._camera_of(trial, feature)\n    x, y, likelihood = _feature(traj, list(traj['featNames']).index(feature))\n    time = self._frame_time(trial, view)           # frame counts can differ between views\n    inside = (time >= T_START) & (time <= T_STOP)\n    return time[inside], x[inside], y[inside], likelihood[inside]\n\ndef _camera_of(self, trial, feature):\n    \"\"\"The camera that tracks a feature, and its tracking for this trial.\"\"\"\n    for view in (SIDE, BOTTOM):\n        traj = self._traj(trial, view)\n        if feature in list(traj['featNames']):     # the name picks out the camera\n            return view, traj\n    raise KeyError('%s is tracked by neither camera' % feature)\n\ndef _feature(traj, index):\n    \"\"\"x, y, and likelihood of one feature, whichever way the file orders ts.\"\"\"\n    ts = np.asarray(traj['ts'])\n    if ts.shape[0] == len(traj['featNames']):      # v7.3 reads it as (features, xyl, frames)\n        return ts[index]\n    return ts[:, :, index].T                       # v5 keeps MATLAB's (frames, xyl, features)\n```\n\niii. Both cameras are used because the tongue is visible in only a small fraction of frames and the two views disagree about which ones — on the example trial the bottom camera tracked it in 192 frames and the side camera in 93 — so using both recovers more of the lick bout than either alone.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *tongue_velocity*?\n\ni. Five steps. **(1)** Frames whose likelihood is at or below 0.9 are dropped; the authors already set x and y to NaN there, so this is the visibility rule already in the data. **(2)** Within each contiguous run of surviving frames, x and y are smoothed with a 5 ms Gaussian and differentiated against the real frame times, and the speed is the magnitude of the two derivatives. **(3)** Each camera's speed is averaged into the 5 ms bins. **(4)** Each is divided by its own 90th percentile over the whole session, then the two are averaged per bin, using whichever view is present when the other is missing. **(5)** The result is split at the session median into two classes, with a third class for bins where neither camera tracked the tongue.\n\nii. Velocity within a run:\n```python\nfor start, stop in _runs(valid):\n    if stop - start < 2:                       # a lone frame has no velocity\n        continue\n    sx = gaussian_filter1d(x[start:stop], sigma, mode='mirror')\n    sy = gaussian_filter1d(y[start:stop], sigma, mode='mirror')\n    speed[start:stop] = np.hypot(np.gradient(sx, time[start:stop]),\n                                 np.gradient(sy, time[start:stop]))\n```\n\nCombining the two cameras:\n```python\ndef tongue_velocity(self):\n    \"\"\"Binned tongue velocity, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n    side = np.array([_bin_frames(*self.side_tongue[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n    bottom = np.array([_bin_frames(*self.bottom_tongue[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n    side = side / self.side_scale\n    bottom = bottom / self.bottom_scale\n    return _mean_over_available(side, bottom)      # one view alone where the other is missing\n```\n\n```python\n_discretize(tongue, np.nanpercentile(tongue, SPLIT_PCT))\n```\n\niii. We combine the information from the two camera view to get the speed estimate. The two views are on different pixel scales — the side camera's 90th percentile is roughly twice the bottom camera's — so they cannot be averaged raw; normalising each by its own percentile is the same device the paper uses for its kinematic overlays, where features are \"standardized by taking the 99th percentile across time and trials\". The 50th-percentile split is the prompt's. The `not visible` class is needed because the tongue is out of view in about 88% of bins.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *tongue_velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The camera runs on its own clock, which starts earlier than the behaviour clock, so `frameTimes` cannot be compared to the go cue directly. The offset is found once per session from the bitcode pulse that both streams record: where it sits in the recording file (`sglx.bitcode.bitstart / sglx.fs`) minus where it sits on the behaviour clock (`bp.ev.bitStart`), taking the mode of each. Frame time from the go cue is then `frameTimes − offset − goCue[trial]`. After that the frames are binned onto the same 5 ms grid as the spikes, so the two streams share one time axis.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef _video_offset(self):\n    \"\"\"Seconds by which the video clock leads the behavior clock (findVideoOffset.m).\"\"\"\n    sample_rate = float(np.ravel(self.obj['sglx']['fs'])[0])\n    video_start = np.ravel(self.obj['sglx']['bitcode']['bitstart']) / sample_rate\n    behavior_start = trial_column(self.obj['bp'], 'ev', 'bitStart')\n    return pd.Series(video_start).mode().iloc[0] - pd.Series(behavior_start).mode().iloc[0]\n\ndef _frame_time(self, trial, view):\n    \"\"\"Frame times of one trial and camera, in seconds from go cue onset.\"\"\"\n    frame_times = np.ravel(self._traj(trial, view)['frameTimes'])\n    return frame_times - self.offset - self.go_cue[trial]\n```\n\niii. This is the reference's `findVideoOffset.m`, and the offset is a session constant so it is computed once in `__init__` rather than per trial. Two checks: after the correction the frames start at −2.475 s from the go cue, just inside the window, and on the example trial the tongue is invisible for the entire delay and appears only after the go cue, with the first protrusion just before the recorded reward time.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *paw_velocity* derived from?\n\ni. The same `obj.traj` tracking, but only the bottom camera and only `top_paw`. The bottom view tracks two paws, `top_paw` and `bottom_paw`; only the first is used.\n\nii.\n```python\nPAW = 'top_paw'                  # this is the reliable view for the paw tracking\n...\nself.paw = self._trial_velocity(PAW)\n```\n\niii. `bottom_paw` drops out through the delay epoch — its likelihood oscillates between 0.2 and 1.0 until about 0.4 s after the go cue, leaving it untracked in roughly half the window — whereas `top_paw` is tracked in essentially every frame. They are two different forepaws rather than two views of one, so they cannot be averaged the way the tongue views are; using the reliably tracked one avoids inventing a delay-epoch signal.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *paw_velocity*?\n\ni. The same velocity computation as the tongue — likelihood cut, per-run Gaussian smoothing of x and y, speed as the magnitude of the two derivatives — then binning into 5 ms bins and a split at the session median, with a third class for untracked bins. No normalisation, since there is only one camera to reconcile, so the values stay in pixels per second.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef paw_velocity(self):\n    \"\"\"Binned paw velocity, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n    return np.array([_bin_frames(*self.paw[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n...\n_discretize(paw, np.nanpercentile(paw, SPLIT_PCT))\n```\n\niii. Normalisation exists only to make two cameras comparable, so it is skipped here. The `not visible` class still appears, on about 19% of bins, since tracking does fail on some trials entirely.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *paw_velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Identically to the tongue: the session's video offset is subtracted from `frameTimes`, then the go cue of that trial, and the frames falling inside the window are binned onto the same 5 ms grid as the spikes. The paw comes from the bottom camera, so its own view's frame times are used.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef _frame_time(self, trial, view):\n    \"\"\"Frame times of one trial and camera, in seconds from go cue onset.\"\"\"\n    frame_times = np.ravel(self._traj(trial, view)['frameTimes'])\n    return frame_times - self.offset - self.go_cue[trial]\n```\n\nTrimmed to the window in `_track`:\n```python\ntime = self._frame_time(trial, view)           # frame counts can differ between views\ninside = (time >= T_START) & (time <= T_STOP)\nreturn time[inside], x[inside], y[inside], likelihood[inside]\n```\n\niii. Same offset and same grid as every other stream, so the paw needs no separate treatment. The frame times are taken from the camera that tracks the feature rather than always the side camera, because in one trial of one session the two cameras recorded different numbers of frames.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *motion_energy* derived from?\n\ni. A separate file, `motionEnergy_<anm>_<date>.mat`, sitting beside the data structure. It holds one trace per trial, with one value per camera frame. Some sessions also carry a copy in `obj.me`, but the standalone file is used since it exists for all 44 sessions while `obj.me` is present in only 28.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef load_motion_energy(name):\n    \"\"\"Motion energy of each trial, one value per camera frame.\"\"\"\n    me = scipy.io.loadmat(session_file(name, 'motionEnergy'), simplify_cells=True)['me']\n    while isinstance(me, dict):              # most files wrap it once, three wrap it twice\n        me = me['data']\n    return me\n```\n\niii. Three different layouts occur across the 44 files — a bare cell array in four, `{data, moveThresh}` in thirty-seven, and `{data: {data, moveThresh}}` in three — so the wrapper is unwrapped in a loop rather than indexed once. The reference's `loadMotionEnergy.m` has the same guard, `if isstruct(me.data), me.data = me.data.data; end`. Where both copies exist they were verified identical on every trial.\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *motion_energy*?\n\ni. None beyond binning. The value is already a single number per frame — the paper computes it per pixel as the difference of the median over the next and previous five frames, then reduces each frame to its 99th percentile across pixels — so there is nothing to smooth, differentiate, or combine. The trace is averaged into the 5 ms bins and split at the session median.\n\nii.\n```python\n_discretize(energy, np.nanpercentile(energy, SPLIT_PCT))\n```\n\niii. The spatial reduction has already been done upstream, so re-deriving anything would only discard information.\n\n## 9-d. How is `output` *motion_energy* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same offset and same grid as the tracking. Motion energy has exactly one value per frame of the side camera, so its frame times are that camera's, corrected by the session offset and the trial's go cue, then trimmed to the window and binned.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef motion_energy(self):\n    \"\"\"Binned motion energy, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n    rows = []\n    for trial in self.trials:\n        time = self._frame_time(trial, SIDE)       # motion energy follows the side camera\n        inside = (time >= T_START) & (time <= T_STOP)\n        rows.append(_bin_frames(time[inside], self.energy[trial][inside]))\n    return np.array(rows)\n```\n\niii. Same as above, motion energy uses the camera frames time index.\n\n## 10. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Three cases, all handled by keeping the trial and marking the gap rather than by filling anything in. **Missing frame times:** three trials in the whole dataset have `frameTimes` entirely NaN, so no frame can be placed on the go-cue clock; `_velocity` returns early and those trials come out as 1000 `not visible` bins for the tongue and paw. **Untracked frames:** wherever DeepLabCut's likelihood is at or below 0.9 the coordinates are already NaN, and those bins get the `not visible` class. **Mismatched frame counts:** in one trial the two cameras recorded different numbers of frames, so each feature is timed by its own camera rather than by the side camera.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef _velocity(self, track):\n    \"\"\"Combined velocity over the valid frames, NaN elsewhere.\"\"\"\n    time, x, y, likelihood = track\n    speed = np.full(time.size, np.nan)\n    if time.size < 2:                              # a few trials have no frame times\n        return time, speed\n```\n\niii. Nothing is interpolated or nearest-filled, because a missing camera frame is genuinely missing information and inventing a velocity for it would put a fabricated class into the output. The `not visible` class exists exactly so those bins can be represented honestly; the format forbids NaN, so some code has to be assigned. Trials with missing video are kept rather than dropped, since their neural, behavioural, and motion-energy data are unaffected.\n\n## 11-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. Reading the files. Loading each session dominates its runtime; everything computed afterwards — spike binning, velocities, discretisation — is cheap by comparison. The whole conversion runs in about 135 s.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef load_mat(path):\n    \"\"\"Load a data_structure .mat as nested dicts (files are either v7.3 HDF5 or v5).\"\"\"\n```\n\niii. The data has to be read once either way, so this is not reducible.\n\n## 11-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. Three loops remain over trials — the per-trial velocity in `_trial_velocity`, and the binning inside `tongue_velocity`, `paw_velocity`, and `motion_energy` — plus one over clusters in `rates`. They stay as loops because each trial has a different number of camera frames, so there is no rectangular array to operate on. The one place a per-trial loop was avoidable is the spike counting, which is a single `histogram2d` over all trials at once.\n\nii.\n```python\ncounts, _, _ = np.histogram2d(spike_trial, spike_time, bins=[trial_edges, BIN_EDGES])\n```\n\niii. Since loading dominates the runtime, vectorising the remaining loops would not measurably change it.\n\n## 11-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. Nothing is recomputed. Each file is read once, the video offset is computed once per session in `Camera.__init__` rather than per trial, each feature's frame-resolution velocity is computed once and reused by the binning and by the percentile, and the bin grid is built once at module level and shared by every trial, session, and stream.\n\nii.\n```python\nself.offset = self._video_offset()             # one constant for the whole session\n...\nself.side_tongue = self._trial_velocity(SIDE_TONGUE)\nself.bottom_tongue = self._trial_velocity(BOTTOM_TONGUE)\nself.paw = self._trial_velocity(PAW)\n```\n\niii. The percentiles and thresholds are session-wide, so the per-trial velocities have to exist before they can be computed.\n\n## 11-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. Only in the reading. `load_mat` walks the whole `obj` tree, so it materialises fields the conversion never touches — `sglx`'s per-trial index arrays, `clu.spkWavs` and `clu.tm`, and the tracked features other than the tongue and paw. Everything computed after loading ends up in the output.\n\nii.\n```python\nif node.dtype == object:                                # cell array / struct-array field\n    return [_h5(f, f[r]) for r in np.asarray(node[()]).ravel()]\n```\n\niii. No unnecessary processing otherwise.\n\n## 11-e. How is memory usage optimized?\n\ni. Firing rates are stored as `float32` and the outputs as `int8` rather than the default `float64`. Each session's `obj` is dropped when `process_session` returns, so only the per-trial arrays are retained. The full output is 2.7 GB, essentially all of it the neural data.\n\nii.\n```python\nreturn np.asarray(rates, np.float32)\n...\nout = np.empty((n_trials, len(OUTPUT_NAMES), N_BINS), np.int8)\n```\n\niii. `float32` halves the largest object in the output at no cost, since firing rates are multiples of 200 Hz and well within its precision, and the outputs are small non-negative integers so `int8` is sufficient. Peak memory is set by the accumulated result rather than by any single session, so nothing further was needed.\n","numLines":582,"startLine":1,"totalLines":582}}}

           return None, None\n   201→            if mask.any():\n   202→                valid_idx = np.where(~mask)[0]\n   203→                for idx in np.where(mask)[0]:\n   204→                    nearest = valid_idx[np.argmin(np.abs(valid_idx - idx))]\n   205→                    xy[idx, col] = xy[nearest, col]\n   206→\n   207→    dx = np.diff(xy[:, 0])\n   208→    dy = np.diff(xy[:, 1])\n   209→    vel = np.sqrt(dx**2 + dy**2) * VIDEO_FPS\n   210→    vel = np.concatenate([[vel[0]], vel])\n   211→    # Set velocity to NaN where not visible\n   212→    vel[~visible] = np.nan\n   213→    return vel, visible\n   214→\n   215→\n   216→# ─── Loading: HDF5 format ──────────────────────────────────────────────────\n   217→\n   218→def load_session_h5(fpath, probe_list):\n   219→    \"\"\"Load session from HDF5 (MATLAB v7.3) file.\"\"\"\n   220→    f = h5py.File(fpath, 'r')\n   221→    obj = f['obj']\n   222→    bp = obj['bp']\n   223→    ev = bp['ev']\n   224→\n   225→ 234→    goCue = np.array(ev['goCue']).flatten()\n   235→\n   236→    # Video offset\n   237→    sglx = obj['sglx']\n   238→    bitcode_bs = np.array(sglx['bitcode']['bitstart']).flatten()\n   239→ keepdims=False).mode\n   242→\n   243→    # Load clusters\n   244→    clu_data = obj['clu']\n   245→    all_spike_trials = []\n   246→    all_spike_trialtm = []\n   247→    all_qualities = []\n all_spike_trialtm.append(spike_trialtm)\n   270→            all_qualities.append(q_str)\n   271→\n   272→    # Load trajectory (bottom cam for tongue/paw)\n   273→    traj_data = obj['traj']\n   274→ else None\n   286→\n   287→    frameTimes_ds = bottom['frameTimes']\n   288→    ts_ds = bottom['ts']\n   289→\n   290→    trial_frame_times = []\n   291→    trial_tongue_xy = []\n   292→      trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n   307→        if paw_idx is not None:\n   308→            trial_paw_xy.append(ts[paw_idx, :2, :].T)\n   309→            trial_paw_conf.append(ts[paw_idx, 2, :])\n 327→\n   328→# ─── Loading: scipy format (MATLAB v5) ─────────────────────────────────────\n   329→\n   330→def _deep_squeeze(x):\n   331→    \"\"\"Recursively unwrap nested object arrays to get the numeric array.\"\"\"\n   332→    while isinstance(x, np.ndarray) and x.dtype == object:\n   333→        if x.size == 1:\n   334→            x = x.flat[0]\n   335→        else:\n   336→            # Try to convert object array of numeric arrays to a flat numeric array\n   337→            try:\n   338→                x = np.concatenate([np.asarray(item).flatten() for item in x.flat])\n   339→            except Exception:\n   340→                break\n   341→    if isinstance(x, np.ndarray):\n   342→        return x.flatten()\n   343→    return np.atleast_1d(x).flatten()\n   344→\n   345→\n   346→def _get_struct_field(struct, field):\n   347→    \"\"\"Safely access a field from a scipy-loaded MATLAB struct.\"\"\"\n   348→    val = struct[field]\n   349→    # struct fields from squeeze_me=False come as (1,1) object arrays\n   350→    if isinstance(val, np.ndarray) and val.dtype == object and val.shape == (1, 1):\n   351→        val = val[0, 0]\n   352→    return val\n   353→\n   354→\n   355→def _unwrap_struct(x):\n   356→    \"\"\"Unwrap (1,1) struct arrays until we reach the actual struct.\"\"\"\n   357→    while isinstance(x, np.ndarray) and x.dtype.names and x.shape == (1, 1):\n   358→        x = x[0, 0]\n   359→    return x\n   360→\n   361→\n   362→def load_session_scipy(fpath, probe_list):\n   363→    \"\"\"Load session from MATLAB v5 .mat file using scipy.\"\"\"\n   364→    d = sio.loadmat(fpath, squeeze_me=False)\n   365→    obj = d['obj']\n   366→    bp = _unwrap_struct(obj['bp'][0, 0])\n   367→    ev = _unwrap_struct(bp['ev'][0, 0])\n   368→\n   369→    ntrials = int(_deep_squeeze(bp['Ntrials'])[0])\n   370→    hit = _deep_squeeze(bp['hit']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n   371→    miss = _deep_squeeze(bp['miss']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n   372→    no = _deep_squeeze(bp['no']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n   373→    R = _deep_squeeze(bp['R']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n   374→    L = _deep_squeeze(bp['L']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n   375→    autowater = _deep_squeeze(bp['autowater']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n   376→    early = _deep_squeeze(bp['early']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n   377→    stim_struct = _unwrap_struct(bp['stim'][0, 0])\n   378→    stim_enable = _deep_squeeze(stim_struct['enable']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n   379→    goCue = _deep_squeeze(ev['goCue']).astype(float)\n   380→\n vidshift = scipy_mode(bitcode_bs, keepdims=False).mode / sglx_fs - scipy_mode(ev_bs, keepdims=False).mode\n   388→\n   389→    # Load clusters\n   390→    # obj['clu'] is a MATLAB cell array {1 x nProbes}\n   391→    # In scipy: obj['clu'][0,0] is either:\n   392→    #   - (1, nProbes) object array: cell array of probes\n   393→    #   - (1, 1) object wrapping a struct array: single probe, extra wrapper\n   394→    clu_raw = obj['clu'][0, 0]\n   395→    # Remove one layer of (1,1) object wrapping if present\n   396→    if isinstance(clu_raw, np.ndarray) and clu_raw.dtype == object and clu_raw.shape == (1, 1):\n   397→        inner = clu_raw[0, 0]\n   398→        if isinstance(inner, np.ndarray) and inner.dtype.names is not None:\n   399→            # Direct struct array for single probe - wrap in cell-like form\n   400→            clu_cell_probes = [inner]\n   401→        elif isinstance(inner, np.ndarray) and inner.dtype == object:\n   402→            # It's the actual cell array\n   403→            clu_cell_probes = [inner[0, pi] for pi in range(inner.shape[1])]\n   404→        else:\n   405→            clu_cell_probes = [inner]\n   406→    elif isinstance(clu_raw, np.ndarray) and clu_raw.dtype == object:\n   407→        clu_cell_probes = [clu_raw[0, pi] for pi in range(clu_raw.shape[1])]\n   408→    elif isinstance(clu_raw, np.ndarray) and clu_raw.dtype.names is not None:\n   409→        clu_cell_probes = [clu_raw]\n   410→    else:\n   411→        clu_cell_probes = []\n   412→\n   413→    all_spike_trials = []\n   414→    all_spike_trialtm = []\n   415→    all_qualities = []\n   416→\n   417→    for probe_num in probe_list:\n   418→        probe_idx = probe_num - 1\n   419→        if probe_idx >= len(clu_cell_probes):\n   420→            continue\n   421→        probe_data = clu_cell_probes[probe_idx]\n   422→\n   423→        if not isinstance(probe_data, np.ndarray) or probe_data.size == 0:\n   424→            continue\n   425→        if probe_data.dtype.names is None:\n   426→            continue\n   427→\n   428→        # probe_data is a struct array (1, n_clusters) or (n_clusters,)\n   429→        if probe_data.ndim == 0:\n   430→            n_clusters = 1\n   431→            cluster_items = [probe_data.item()]\n   432→        elif probe_data.ndim == 1:\n   433→            n_clusters = probe_data.shape[0]\n   434→ all_qualities.append(q_str)\n   453→\n   454→    # Load trajectory (bottom cam)\n   455→    # traj is a {2,1} or {1,2} cell array: [side_cam; bottom_cam]\n   456→    traj_raw = obj['traj'][0, 0]\n   457→    # Unwrap one level of (1,1) object if needed\n   458→    if isinstance(traj_raw, np.ndarray) and traj_raw.dtype == object and traj_raw.shape == (1, 1):\n   459→        traj_raw = traj_raw[0, 0]\n   460→    # traj_raw should now be (1,2) or (2,1) object array or (1,nTrials) struct array\n   461→    if isinstance(traj_raw, np.ndarray) and traj_raw.dtype == object:\n   462→        # Find 466→            bottom_cam = traj_raw[1, 0]\n   467→        else:\n   468→            bottom_cam = traj_raw[0, 1] if traj_raw.shape[1] >= 2 else traj_raw[1, 0]\n   469→    else:\n   470→        bottom_cam = traj_raw  # Hopefully struct array directly\n   471→    # Unwrap one more level if needed\n   472→    if isinstance(bottom_cam, np.ndarray) and bottom_cam.dtype == object and bottom_cam.shape == (1, 1):\n   473→        bottom_cam = bottom_cam[0, 0]\n   474→\n   475→    # Get feature names from first trial\n   476→    def _get_trial_struct(bc, idx):\n   477→        \"\"\"Get the struct for trial idx from bottom_cam array.\"\"\"\n   478→        if bc.dtype.names:\n   479→            return bc[0, idx] if bc.ndim > 1 else bc[idx]\n   480→        return bc[0, idx]\n   481→\n   482→    first_trial = _get_trial_struct(bottom_cam, 0)\n   483→    feat_names_raw = first_trial['featNames']\n   484→    # Unwrap nested object arrays\n   485→    while 488→    if isinstance(feat_names_raw, np.ndarray):\n   489→        for item in feat_names_raw.flat:\n   490→            if isinstance(item, np.ndarray):\n   491→                item = trial_tongue_conf = []\n   500→    trial_paw_xy = []\n   501→    trial_paw_conf = []\n   502→\n   503→    for t in range(ntrials):\n   504→        trial_s = _get_trial_struct(bottom_cam, t)\n   505→        ft_raw = trial_s['frameTimes']\n   506→        ft = _deep_squeeze(ft_raw).astype(float)\n   507→        trial_frame_times.append(ft)\n   508→\n   509→        ts_raw = trial_s['ts']\n   510→        while isinstance(ts_raw, np.ndarray) and ts_raw.dtype == object and ts_raw.size == 1:\n   511→            ts_raw = ts_raw.flat[0]\n   512→        ts = np.asarray(ts_raw, dtype=float)\n   513→\n   514→        if ts.ndim == 3:\n   515→            n_feats = len(bottom_feat_names)\n   516→            if ts.shape[0] == n_feats:\n   517→                # (n_bodyparts, 3, n_frames)\n   518→                if tongue_idx is not None:\n   519→                    trial_tongue_xy.append(ts[tongue_idx, :2, :].T)\n   520→                    trial_tongue_conf.append(ts[tongue_idx, 2, :])\n   521→                else:\n   522→                    trial_tongue_xy.append(None)\n   523→                    trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n   524→                if paw_idx is not None:\n   525→                    trial_paw_xy.append(ts[paw_idx, :2, :].T)\n   526→                    trial_paw_conf.append(ts[paw_idx, 2, :])\n   527→                else:\n   528→                    trial_paw_xy.append(None)\n   529→                    trial_paw_conf.append(None)\n   530→            elif ts.shape[2] == n_feats:\n   531→                # (n_frames, 3, n_bodyparts)\n   532→                if tongue_idx is not             trial_tongue_xy.append(None)\n   537→                    trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n   538→                if paw_idx is not None:\n   539→                    trial_paw_xy.append(ts[:, :2, paw_idx])\n   540→                    trial_paw_conf.append(ts[:, 2, paw_idx])\n   541→                else:\n   542→                    trial_paw_xy.append(None)\n   543→                    trial_paw_conf.append(None)\n   544→            else:\n   545→                trial_tongue_xy.append(None)\n   546→                trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n   547→                trial_paw_xy.append(None)\n   548→                trial_paw_conf.append(None)\n   549→        else:\n   550→            trial_tongue_xy.append(None)\n   551→            trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n   552→            trial_paw_xy.append(None)\n   553→            trial_paw_conf.append(None)\n   554→\n   555→    return {\n   556→        'ntrials': ntrials, 'hit': 'spike_trials': all_spike_trials, 'spike_trialtm': all_spike_trialtm,\n   560→        'qualities': all_qualities,\n   561→        'trial_frame_times': trial_frame_times,\n   562→        'trial_tongue_xy': trial_tongue_xy, 'trial_tongue_conf': trial_tongue_conf,\n   563→        'trial_paw_xy': trial_paw_xy, 'trial_paw_conf': trial_paw_conf,\n   564→    }\n   565→\n   566→\n   567→def load_session(anm, date, probe_list, data_dir):\n   568→    \"\"\"Load one session, auto-detecting file format.\"\"\"\n   569→    fn = f'data_structure_{anm}_{date}.mat'\n   570→    fpath = os.path.join(data_dir, fn)\n   571→\n   572→    # Try HDF5 first\n   573→    try:\n   574→        sess = load_session_h5(fpath, probe_list)\n   575→    except Exception:\n   576→        # Fall back to scipy\n   577→        sess = load_session_scipy(fpath, probe_list)\n   578→\n   579→    sess['anm'] = anm\n   580→    sess['date'] = date\n   581→\n   582→    # Load motion energy\n   583→    me_fn = f'motionEnergy_{anm}_{date}.mat'\n   584→    me_fpath = os.path.join(data_dir, me_fn)\n   585→    sess['me_data'] = None\n   586→    sess['me_thresh'] = None\n   587→\n   588→    if os.path.exists(me_fpath):\n   589→        try:\n   590→            me_mat = sio.loadmat(me_fpath, squeeze_me=True)\n   591→            me = me_mat['me']\n   592→            me_data_raw = me['data'].item()\n   593→            sess['me_thresh'] = float(me['moveThresh'].item())\n   594→            sess['me_data'] = [me_data_raw[i] for i in range(len(me_data_raw))]\n   595→        except Exception:\n   596→            try:\n   597→                mf = h5py.File(me_fpath, 'r')\n   598→                me_g = mf['me']\n   599→                sess['me_thresh'] = float(np.array(me_g['moveThresh']).flatten()[0])\n   600→                me_data_ds = me_g['data']\n   601→                sess['me_data'] = []\n   602→                for t in range(me_data_ds.shape[0]):\n   603→                    ref = me_data_ds[t, 0]\n   604→                    sess['me_data'].append(np.array(mf[ref]).flatten())\n   605→                mf.close()\n   606→            except Exception:\n   607→                pass\n   608→\n   609→    return sess\n   610→\n   611→\n   612→def process_session(sess):\n   613→    \"\"\"Process one session: bin spikes, align, smooth, filter, extract outputs.\"\"\"\n   614→    edges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT)\n   615→    time_centers = edges[:-1] + DT / 2\n   616→    n_timebins = len(time_centers)\n   617→\n   618→    goCue = sess['goCue']\n   619→    ntrials = sess['ntrials']\n   620→\n   621→    # Trial mask: exclude stim and early lick\n   622→    trial_mask = ~sess['stim_enable'] & ~sess['early']\n   623→    valid_trials = np.where(trial_mask)[0]\n   624→\n   625→    if len(valid_trials) < 2:\n   626→        print(f\"  Skipping {sess['anm']}_{sess['date']}: too few valid trials ({len(valid_trials)})\")\n   627→        return None\n   628→\n   629→    # Bin and smooth spikes for each unit, all trials\n   630→    n_units = len(sess['spike_trials'])\n   631→    trialdat = np.zeros((n_timebins, n_units, ntrials))\n   632→\n   633→    for ui in range(n_units):\n   634→        spike_trials_raw = sess['spike_trials'][ui]\n   635→        spike_trialtm_raw = sess['spike_trialtm'][ui]\n   636→\n   637→        for ti in range(ntrials):\n   638→            spk_mask = spike_trials_raw == (ti + 1)\n   639→            if not np.any(spk_mask):\n   640→                continue\n   641→            aligned_times = spike_trialtm_raw[spk_mask] - goCue[ti]\n   642→            counts, _ = np.histogram(aligned_times, bins=edges)\n   643→            fr = counts / DT\n   644→            trialdat[:, ui, ti] = smooth_data(fr, SMOOTH_WINDOW, BC_TYPE)\n   645→\n   646→    # Remove low FR units\n   647→    mean_frs = np.mean(trialdat[:, :, valid_trials], axis=(0, 2))\n   648→    good_units = mean_frs > LOW_FR_THRESH\n   649→\n   650→    if good_units.sum() < MIN_UNITS:\n   651→        print(f\"  Skipping {sess['anm']}_{sess['date']}: only {good_units.sum()} units after FR filter\")\n   652→        return None\n   653→\n   654→    trialdat = trialdat[:, good_units, :]\n   655→    n_units_final = trialdat.shape[1]\n   656→    print(f\" 10 ms bins\nSMOOTH_WINDOW = 15  # bins for causal Gaussian kernel\nBC_TYPE = 'reflect'\nLOW_FR_THRESH = 1.0  # Hz\nMIN_UNITS = 10  # minimum units per session\nEXCLUDED_QUALITIES = {'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'}\nVIDEO_FPS = 400  # Hz\n\n\n# ─── Helper functions ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\ndef read_h5_string(f, ref):\n    \"\"\"Read a MATLAB string stored as uint16 character codes via HDF5 reference.\"\"\"\n    data = f[ref][:]\n    return ''.join(chr(int(c)) for c in data.flatten())\n\n\ndef causal_gaussian_kernel(N):\n    \"\"\"Replicate MATLAB mySmooth: gausswin(N) with first half zeroed (causal).\"\"\"\n    alpha = 2.5\n    n = np.arange(N)\n    w = np.exp(-0.5 * (alpha * (n - (N - 1) / 2) / ((N - 1) / 2)) ** 2)\n    w[:N // 2] = 0\n    w /= w.sum()\n    return w\n\n\ndef smooth_data(x, N, bc_type='reflect'):\n    \"\"\"Smooth 1D or 2D array along first axis using causal Gaussian kernel.\"\"\"\n    if N <= 1:\n        return x\n\n    kern = causal_gaussian_kernel(N)\n\n    was_1d = x.ndim == 1\n    if was_1d:\n        x = x[:, None]\n\n    if bc_type == 'reflect':\n        x_padded = np.concatenate([x[:N], x], axis=0)\n        trim = N\n    elif bc_type == 'zeropad':\n        x_padded = np.concatenate([np.zeros((N, x.shape[1])), x], axis=0)\n        trim = N\n    else:\n        x_padded = x\n        trim = 0\n\n    out = np.zeros_like(x_padded)\n    for j in range(x_padded.shape[1]):\n        out[:, j] = np.convolve(x_padded[:, j], kern, mode='same')\n    out = out[trim:]\n\n    if was_1d:\n        out = out[:, 0]\n    return out\n\n\ndef fill_nearest(arr):\n    \"\"\"Fill NaN values with nearest non-NaN value along axis 0 for each column.\"\"\"\n    if arr.ndim == 1:\n        nans = np.isnan(arr)\n        if nans.all() or not nans.any():\n            return arr\n        valid = np.where(~nans)[0]\n        for idx in np.where(nans)[0]:\n            nearest = valid[np.argmin(np.abs(valid - idx))]\n            arr[idx] = arr[nearest]\n    elif arr.ndim == 2:\n        for col in range(arr.shape[1]):\n            fill_nearest(arr[:, col])\n    return arr\n\n\nDLC_CONF_THRESH = 0.9  # Confidence threshold for DLC visibility\n\n\ndef compute_velocity(xy_coords, confidence=None, fill_missing=True):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute velocity magnitude from (nframes, 2) position data at 400 Hz.\n    Returns (velocity, visible_mask).\n    If confidence is provided, frames below DLC_CONF_THRESH are marked not visible.\n    For non-tongue features, fill missing with nearest (matching MATLAB pipeline).\n    For tongue, do NOT fill missing (per methods: \"except for the tongue\").\n    \"\"\"\n    if xy_coords is None or len(xy_coords) < 2:\n        return None, None\n    xy = xy_coords.copy().astype(float)\n\n    # Determine visibility from DLC confidence\n    if confidence is not None:\n        visible = confidence >= DLC_CONF_THRESH\n    else:\n        visible = np.ones(len(xy), dtype=bool)\n\n    if fill_missing:\n        # Fill missing/NaN values with nearest (for non-tongue features)\n        for col in range(xy.shape[1]):\n            mask = np.isnan(xy[:, col])\n            if mask.all():\n                return None, None\n            if mask.any():\n                valid_idx = np.where(~mask)[0]\n                for idx in np.where(mask)[0]:\n                    nearest = valid_idx[np.argmin(np.abs(valid_idx - idx))]\n                    xy[idx, col] = xy[nearest, col]\n\n    dx = np.diff(xy[:, 0])\n    dy = np.diff(xy[:, 1])\n    vel = np.sqrt(dx**2 + dy**2) * VIDEO_FPS\n    vel = np.concatenate([[vel[0]], vel])\n    # Set velocity to NaN where not visible\n    vel[~visible] = np.nan\n    return vel, visible\n\n\n# ─── Loading: HDF5 format ──────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\ndef load_session_h5(fpath, probe_list):\n    \"\"\"Load session from HDF5 (MATLAB v7.3) file.\"\"\"\n    f = h5py.File(fpath, 'r')\n    obj = f['obj']\n    bp = obj['bp']\n    ev = bp['ev']\n\n    ntrials = int(bp['Ntrials'][0, 0])\n    hit = np.array(bp['hit']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    miss = np.array(bp['miss']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    no = np.array(bp['no']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    R = np.array(bp['R']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    L = np.array(bp['L']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    autowater = np.array(bp['autowater']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    early = np.array(bp['early']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    stim_enable = np.array(bp['stim']['enable']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    goCue = np.array(ev['goCue']).flatten()\n\n    # Video offset\n    sglx = obj['sglx']\n    bitcode_bs = np.array(sglx['bitcode']['bitstart']).flatten()\n    sglx_fs = np.array(sglx['fs']).flatten()[0]\n    ev_bs = np.array(ev['bitStart']).flatten()\n    vidshift = scipy_mode(bitcode_bs, keepdims=False).mode / sglx_fs - scipy_mode(ev_bs, keepdims=False).mode\n\n    # Load clusters\n    clu_data = obj['clu']\n    all_spike_trials = []\n    all_spike_trialtm = []\n    all_qualities = []\n\n    for probe_num in probe_list:\n        probe_idx = probe_num - 1\n        probe_ref = clu_data[probe_idx, 0]\n        probe_group = f[probe_ref]\n\n        if isinstance(probe_group, h5py.Dataset):\n            continue\n\n        quality_ds = probe_group['quality']\n        trial_ds = probe_group['trial']\n        trialtm_ds = probe_group['trialtm']\n        n_clusters = quality_ds.shape[0]\n\n        for ci in range(n_clusters):\n            q_str = read_h5_string(f, quality_ds[ci, 0]).strip()\n            if q_str.lower() in {q.lower() for q in EXCLUDED_QUALITIES}:\n                continue\n            spike_trials = np.array(f[trial_ds[ci, 0]]).flatten()\n            spike_trialtm = np.array(f[trialtm_ds[ci, 0]]).flatten()\n            all_spike_trials.append(spike_trials)\n            all_spike_trialtm.append(spike_trialtm)\n            all_qualities.append(q_str)\n\n    # Load trajectory (bottom cam for tongue/paw)\n    traj_data = obj['traj']\n    bottom_ref = traj_data[1, 0]\n    bottom = f[bottom_ref]\n\n    # Feature names\n    feat_names_ref = bottom['featNames'][0, 0]\n    feat_names_ds = f[feat_names_ref]\n    bottom_feat_names = []\n    for j in range(feat_names_ds.shape[1]):\n        bottom_feat_names.append(read_h5_string(f, feat_names_ds[0, j]))\n\n    tongue_idx = bottom_feat_names.index('top_tongue') if 'top_tongue' in bottom_feat_names else None\n    paw_idx = bottom_feat_names.index('top_paw') if 'top_paw' in bottom_feat_names else None\n\n    frameTimes_ds = bottom['frameTimes']\n    ts_ds = bottom['ts']\n\n    trial_frame_times = []\n    trial_tongue_xy = []\n    trial_tongue_conf = []\n    trial_paw_xy = []\n    trial_paw_conf = []\n\n    for t in range(ntrials):\n        ft = np.array(f[frameTimes_ds[t, 0]]).flatten()\n        trial_frame_times.append(ft)\n\n        ts = np.array(f[ts_ds[t, 0]])  # (n_bodyparts, 3, n_frames)\n        if tongue_idx is not None:\n            trial_tongue_xy.append(ts[tongue_idx, :2, :].T)\n            trial_tongue_conf.append(ts[tongue_idx, 2, :])\n        else:\n            trial_tongue_xy.append(None)\n            trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n        if paw_idx is not None:\n            trial_paw_xy.append(ts[paw_idx, :2, :].T)\n            trial_paw_conf.append(ts[paw_idx, 2, :])\n        else:\n            trial_paw_xy.append(None)\n            trial_paw_conf.append(None)\n\n    f.close()\n\n    return {\n        'ntrials': ntrials, 'hit': hit, 'miss': miss, 'no': no,\n        'R': R, 'L': L, 'autowater': autowater, 'early': early,\n        'stim_enable': stim_enable, 'goCue': goCue, 'vidshift': vidshift,\n        'spike_trials': all_spike_trials, 'spike_trialtm': all_spike_trialtm,\n        'qualities': all_qualities,\n        'trial_frame_times': trial_frame_times,\n        'trial_tongue_xy': trial_tongue_xy, 'trial_tongue_conf': trial_tongue_conf,\n        'trial_paw_xy': trial_paw_xy, 'trial_paw_conf': trial_paw_conf,\n    }\n\n\n# ─── Loading: scipy format (MATLAB v5) ─────────────────────────────────────\n\ndef _deep_squeeze(x):\n    \"\"\"Recursively unwrap nested object arrays to get the numeric array.\"\"\"\n    while isinstance(x, np.ndarray) and x.dtype == object:\n        if x.size == 1:\n            x = x.flat[0]\n        else:\n            # Try to convert object array of numeric arrays to a flat numeric array\n            try:\n                x = np.concatenate([np.asarray(item).flatten() for item in x.flat])\n            except Exception:\n                break\n    if isinstance(x, np.ndarray):\n        return x.flatten()\n    return np.atleast_1d(x).flatten()\n\n\ndef _get_struct_field(struct, field):\n    \"\"\"Safely access a field from a scipy-loaded MATLAB struct.\"\"\"\n    val = struct[field]\n    # struct fields from squeeze_me=False come as (1,1) object arrays\n    if isinstance(val, np.ndarray) and val.dtype == object and val.shape == (1, 1):\n        val = val[0, 0]\n    return val\n\n\ndef _unwrap_struct(x):\n    \"\"\"Unwrap (1,1) struct arrays until we reach the actual struct.\"\"\"\n    while isinstance(x, np.ndarray) and x.dtype.names and x.shape == (1, 1):\n        x = x[0, 0]\n    return x\n\n\ndef load_session_scipy(fpath, probe_list):\n    \"\"\"Load session from MATLAB v5 .mat file using scipy.\"\"\"\n    d = sio.loadmat(fpath, squeeze_me=False)\n    obj = d['obj']\n    bp = _unwrap_struct(obj['bp'][0, 0])\n    ev = _unwrap_struct(bp['ev'][0, 0])\n\n    ntrials = int(_deep_squeeze(bp['Ntrials'])[0])\n    hit = _deep_squeeze(bp['hit']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n    miss = _deep_squeeze(bp['miss']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n    no = _deep_squeeze(bp['no']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n    R = _deep_squeeze(bp['R']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n    L = _deep_squeeze(bp['L']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n    autowater = _deep_squeeze(bp['autowater']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n    early = _deep_squeeze(bp['early']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n    stim_struct = _unwrap_struct(bp['stim'][0, 0])\n    stim_enable = _deep_squeeze(stim_struct['enable']).astype(float).astype(bool)\n    goCue = _deep_squeeze(ev['goCue']).astype(float)\n\n    # Video offset\n    sglx = _unwrap_struct(obj['sglx'][0, 0])\n    bitcode = _unwrap_struct(sglx['bitcode'][0, 0])\n    bitcode_bs = _deep_squeeze(bitcode['bitstart']).astype(float)\n    sglx_fs = float(_deep_squeeze(sglx['fs']).astype(float)[0])\n    ev_bs = _deep_squeeze(ev['bitStart']).astype(float)\n    vidshift = scipy_mode(bitcode_bs, keepdims=False).mode / sglx_fs - scipy_mode(ev_bs, keepdims=False).mode\n\n    # Load clusters\n    # obj['clu'] is a MATLAB cell array {1 x nProbes}\n    # In scipy: obj['clu'][0,0] is either:\n    #   - (1, nProbes) object array: cell array of probes\n    #   - (1, 1) object wrapping a struct array: single probe, extra wrapper\n    clu_raw = obj['clu'][0, 0]\n    # Remove one layer of (1,1) object wrapping if present\n    if isinstance(clu_raw, np.ndarray) and clu_raw.dtype == object and clu_raw.shape == (1, 1):\n        inner = clu_raw[0, 0]\n        if isinstance(inner, np.ndarray) and inner.dtype.names is not None:\n            # Direct struct array for single probe - wrap in cell-like form\n            clu_cell_probes = [inner]\n        elif isinstance(inner, np.ndarray) and inner.dtype == object:\n            # It's the actual cell array\n            clu_cell_probes = [inner[0, pi] for pi in range(inner.shape[1])]\n        else:\n            clu_cell_probes = [inner]\n    elif isinstance(clu_raw, np.ndarray) and clu_raw.dtype == object:\n        clu_cell_probes = [clu_raw[0, pi] for pi in range(clu_raw.shape[1])]\n    elif isinstance(clu_raw, np.ndarray) and clu_raw.dtype.names is not None:\n        clu_cell_probes = [clu_raw]\n    else:\n        clu_cell_probes = []\n\n    all_spike_trials = []\n    all_spike_trialtm = []\n    all_qualities = []\n\n    for probe_num in probe_list:\n        probe_idx = probe_num - 1\n        if probe_idx >= len(clu_cell_probes):\n            continue\n        probe_data = clu_cell_probes[probe_idx]\n\n        if not isinstance(probe_data, np.ndarray) or probe_data.size == 0:\n            continue\n        if probe_data.dtype.names is None:\n            continue\n\n        # probe_data is a struct array (1, n_clusters) or (n_clusters,)\n        if probe_data.ndim == 0:\n            n_clusters = 1\n            cluster_items = [probe_data.item()]\n        elif probe_data.ndim == 1:\n            n_clusters = probe_data.shape[0]\n            cluster_items = [probe_data[ci] for ci in range(n_clusters)]\n        else:\n            n_clusters = probe_data.shape[1]\n            cluster_items = [probe_data[0, ci] for ci in range(n_clusters)]\n\n        for ci, clu_item in enumerate(cluster_items):\n            q_raw = clu_item['quality']\n            if isinstance(q_raw, np.ndarray):\n                q_str = str(q_raw.flatten()[0]).strip() if q_raw.size > 0 else ''\n            else:\n                q_str = str(q_raw).strip()\n            if q_str.lower() in {q.lower() for q in EXCLUDED_QUALITIES}:\n                continue\n\n            spike_trials = _deep_squeeze(clu_item['trial']).astype(float)\n            spike_trialtm = _deep_squeeze(clu_item['trialtm']).astype(float)\n            all_spike_trials.append(spike_trials)\n            all_spike_trialtm.append(spike_trialtm)\n            all_qualities.append(q_str)\n\n    # Load trajectory (bottom cam)\n    # traj is a {2,1} or {1,2} cell array: [side_cam; bottom_cam]\n    traj_raw = obj['traj'][0, 0]\n    # Unwrap one level of (1,1) object if needed\n    if isinstance(traj_raw, np.ndarray) and traj_raw.dtype == object and traj_raw.shape == (1, 1):\n        traj_raw = traj_raw[0, 0]\n    # traj_raw should now be (1,2) or (2,1) object array or (1,nTrials) struct array\n    if isinstance(traj_raw, np.ndarray) and traj_raw.dtype == object:\n        # Find bottom cam: index 1 in whichever dimension has size 2\n        if traj_raw.shape == (1, 2):\n            bottom_cam = traj_raw[0, 1]\n        elif traj_raw.shape == (2, 1):\n            bottom_cam = traj_raw[1, 0]\n        else:\n            bottom_cam = traj_raw[0, 1] if traj_raw.shape[1] >= 2 else traj_raw[1, 0]\n    else:\n        bottom_cam = traj_raw  # Hopefully struct array directly\n    # Unwrap one more level if needed\n    if isinstance(bottom_cam, np.ndarray) and bottom_cam.dtype == object and bottom_cam.shape == (1, 1):\n        bottom_cam = bottom_cam[0, 0]\n\n    # Get feature names from first trial\n    def _get_trial_struct(bc, idx):\n        \"\"\"Get the struct for trial idx from bottom_cam array.\"\"\"\n        if bc.dtype.names:\n            return bc[0, idx] if bc.ndim > 1 else bc[idx]\n        return bc[0, idx]\n\n    first_trial = _get_trial_struct(bottom_cam, 0)\n    feat_names_raw = first_trial['featNames']\n    # Unwrap nested object arrays\n    while isinstance(feat_names_raw, np.ndarray) and feat_names_raw.dtype == object and feat_names_raw.size == 1:\n        feat_names_raw = feat_names_raw.flat[0]\n    bottom_feat_names = []\n    if isinstance(feat_names_raw, np.ndarray):\n        for item in feat_names_raw.flat:\n            if isinstance(item, np.ndarray):\n                item = str(item.flat[0])\n            bottom_feat_names.append(str(item).strip())\n\n    tongue_idx = bottom_feat_names.index('top_tongue') if 'top_tongue' in bottom_feat_names else None\n    paw_idx = bottom_feat_names.index('top_paw') if 'top_paw' in bottom_feat_names else None\n\n    trial_frame_times = []\n    trial_tongue_xy = []\n    trial_tongue_conf = []\n    trial_paw_xy = []\n    trial_paw_conf = []\n\n    for t in range(ntrials):\n        trial_s = _get_trial_struct(bottom_cam, t)\n        ft_raw = trial_s['frameTimes']\n        ft = _deep_squeeze(ft_raw).astype(float)\n        trial_frame_times.append(ft)\n\n        ts_raw = trial_s['ts']\n        while isinstance(ts_raw, np.ndarray) and ts_raw.dtype == object and ts_raw.size == 1:\n            ts_raw = ts_raw.flat[0]\n        ts = np.asarray(ts_raw, dtype=float)\n\n        if ts.ndim == 3:\n            n_feats = len(bottom_feat_names)\n            if ts.shape[0] == n_feats:\n                # (n_bodyparts, 3, n_frames)\n                if tongue_idx is not None:\n                    trial_tongue_xy.append(ts[tongue_idx, :2, :].T)\n                    trial_tongue_conf.append(ts[tongue_idx, 2, :])\n                else:\n                    trial_tongue_xy.append(None)\n                    trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n                if paw_idx is not None:\n                    trial_paw_xy.append(ts[paw_idx, :2, :].T)\n                    trial_paw_conf.append(ts[paw_idx, 2, :])\n                else:\n                    trial_paw_xy.append(None)\n                    trial_paw_conf.append(None)\n            elif ts.shape[2] == n_feats:\n                # (n_frames, 3, n_bodyparts)\n                if tongue_idx is not None:\n                    trial_tongue_xy.append(ts[:, :2, tongue_idx])\n                    trial_tongue_conf.append(ts[:, 2, tongue_idx])\n                else:\n                    trial_tongue_xy.append(None)\n                    trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n                if paw_idx is not None:\n                    trial_paw_xy.append(ts[:, :2, paw_idx])\n                    trial_paw_conf.append(ts[:, 2, paw_idx])\n                else:\n                    trial_paw_xy.append(None)\n                    trial_paw_conf.append(None)\n            else:\n                trial_tongue_xy.append(None)\n                trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n                trial_paw_xy.append(None)\n                trial_paw_conf.append(None)\n        else:\n            trial_tongue_xy.append(None)\n            trial_tongue_conf.append(None)\n            trial_paw_xy.append(None)\n            trial_paw_conf.append(None)\n\n    return {\n        'ntrials': ntrials, 'hit': hit, 'miss': miss, 'no': no,\n        'R': R, 'L': L, 'autowater': autowater, 'early': early,\n        'stim_enable': stim_enable, 'goCue': goCue, 'vidshift': vidshift,\n        'spike_trials': all_spike_trials, 'spike_trialtm': all_spike_trialtm,\n        'qualities': all_qualities,\n        'trial_frame_times': trial_frame_times,\n        'trial_tongue_xy': trial_tongue_xy, 'trial_tongue_conf': trial_tongue_conf,\n        'trial_paw_xy': trial_paw_xy, 'trial_paw_conf': trial_paw_conf,\n    }\n\n\ndef load_session(anm, date, probe_list, data_dir):\n    \"\"\"Load one session, auto-detecting file format.\"\"\"\n    fn = f'data_structure_{anm}_{date}.mat'\n    fpath = os.path.join(data_dir, fn)\n\n    # Try HDF5 first\n    try:\n        sess = load_session_h5(fpath, probe_list)\n    except Exception:\n        # Fall back to scipy\n        sess = load_session_scipy(fpath, probe_list)\n\n    sess['anm'] = anm\n    sess['date'] = date\n\n    # Load motion energy\n    me_fn = f'motionEnergy_{anm}_{date}.mat'\n    me_fpath = os.path.join(data_dir, me_fn)\n    sess['me_data'] = None\n    sess['me_thresh'] = None\n\n    if os.path.exists(me_fpath):\n        try:\n            me_mat = sio.loadmat(me_fpath, squeeze_me=True)\n            me = me_mat['me']\n            me_data_raw = me['data'].item()\n            sess['me_thresh'] = float(me['moveThresh'].item())\n            sess['me_data'] = [me_data_raw[i] for i in range(len(me_data_raw))]\n        except Exception:\n            try:\n                mf = h5py.File(me_fpath, 'r')\n                me_g = mf['me']\n                sess['me_thresh'] = float(np.array(me_g['moveThresh']).flatten()[0])\n                me_data_ds = me_g['data']\n                sess['me_data'] = []\n                for t in range(me_data_ds.shape[0]):\n                    ref = me_data_ds[t, 0]\n                    sess['me_data'].append(np.array(mf[ref]).flatten())\n                mf.close()\n            except Exception:\n                pass\n\n    return sess\n\n\ndef process_session(sess):\n    \"\"\"Process one session: bin spikes, align, smooth, filter, extract outputs.\"\"\"\n    edges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT)\n    time_centers = edges[:-1] + DT / 2\n    n_timebins = len(time_centers)\n\n    goCue = sess['goCue']\n    ntrials = sess['ntrials']\n\n    # Trial mask: exclude stim and early lick\n    trial_mask = ~sess['stim_enable'] & ~sess['early']\n    valid_trials = np.where(trial_mask)[0]\n\n    if len(valid_trials) < 2:\n        print(f\"  Skipping {sess['anm']}_{sess['date']}: too few valid trials ({len(valid_trials)})\")\n        return None\n\n    # Bin and smooth spikes for each unit, all trials\n    n_units = len(sess['spike_trials'])\n    trialdat = np.zeros((n_timebins, n_units, ntrials))\n\n    for ui in range(n_units):\n        spike_trials_raw = sess['spike_trials'][ui]\n        spike_trialtm_raw = sess['spike_trialtm'][ui]\n\n        for ti in range(ntrials):\n            spk_mask = spike_trials_raw == (ti + 1)\n            if not np.any(spk_mask):\n                continue\n            aligned_times = spike_trialtm_raw[spk_mask] - goCue[ti]\n            counts, _ = np.histogram(aligned_times, bins=edges)\n            fr = counts / DT\n            trialdat[:, ui, ti] = smooth_data(fr, SMOOTH_WINDOW, BC_TYPE)\n\n    # Remove low FR units\n    mean_frs = np.mean(trialdat[:, :, valid_trials], axis=(0, 2))\n    good_units = mean_frs > LOW_FR_THRESH\n\n    if good_units.sum() < MIN_UNITS:\n        print(f\"  Skipping {sess['anm']}_{sess['date']}: only {good_units.sum()} units after FR filter\")\n        return None\n\n    trialdat = trialdat[:, good_units, :]\n    n_units_final = trialdat.shape[1]\n    print(f\"  {sess['anm']}_{sess['date']}: {n_units_final} units, {len(valid_trials)} trials\")\n\n    # Align video data\n    vidshift = sess['vidshift']\n    taxis = time_centers\n    me_available = sess['me_data'] is not None\n\n    tongue_vel_aligned = np.full((n_timebins, ntrials), np.nan)\n    paw_vel_aligned = np.full((n_timebins, ntrials), np.nan)\n    me_aligned = np.full((n_timebins, ntrials), np.nan)\n    tongue_visible = np.zeros((n_timebins, ntrials), dtype=bool)\n    paw_visible = np.zeros((n_timebins, ntrials), dtype=bool)\n\n    for ti in range(ntrials):\n        ft = sess['trial_frame_times'][ti]\n        if len(ft) == 0:\n            continue\n        ft_aligned = ft - vidshift - goCue[ti]\n\n        # Tongue velocity (do NOT fill missing - per methods: \"except for the tongue\")\n        tongue_xy = sess['trial_tongue_xy'][ti]\n        tongue_conf = sess['trial_tongue_conf'][ti]\n        if tongue_xy is not None and len(tongue_xy) > 1:\n            vel, vis = compute_velocity(tongue_xy, confidence=tongue_conf, fill_missing=False)\n            if vel is not None:\n                try:\n                    # Interpolate velocity (NaNs where not visible)\n                    f_interp = interp1d(ft_aligned, vel, kind='linear',\n                                        bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n                    v = f_interp(taxis)\n                    tongue_vel_aligned[:, ti] = v\n                    # Interpolate visibility\n                    vis_float = vis.astype(float)\n                    f_vis = interp1d(ft_aligned, vis_float, kind='nearest',\n                                     bounds_error=False, fill_value=0)\n                    tongue_visible[:, ti] = f_vis(taxis) > 0.5\n                except Exception:\n                    pass\n\n        # Paw velocity (fill missing with nearest - per methods)\n        paw_xy = sess['trial_paw_xy'][ti]\n        paw_conf = sess['trial_paw_conf'][ti]\n        if paw_xy is not None and len(paw_xy) > 1:\n            vel, vis = compute_velocity(paw_xy, confidence=paw_conf, fill_missing=True)\n            if vel is not None:\n                try:\n                    f_interp = interp1d(ft_aligned, vel, kind='linear',\n                                        bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n                    v = f_interp(taxis)\n                    paw_vel_aligned[:, ti] = v\n                    f_vis = interp1d(ft_aligned, vis.astype(float), kind='nearest',\n                                     bounds_error=False, fill_value=0)\n                    paw_visible[:, ti] = f_vis(taxis) > 0.5\n                except Exception:\n                    pass\n\n        # Motion energy\n        if me_available and ti < len(sess['me_data']):\n            me_trial = sess['me_data'][ti]\n            if len(me_trial) == len(ft):\n                try:\n                    f_interp = interp1d(ft_aligned, me_trial, kind='linear',\n                                        bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n                    me_aligned[:, ti] = f_interp(taxis)\n                except Exception:\n                    pass\n\n    # Fill NaNs with nearest for paw and ME (not tongue - per methods)\n    fill_nearest(paw_vel_aligned)\n    fill_nearest(me_aligned)\n\n    # Update paw visibility after filling (tongue keeps DLC-based visibility)\n    paw_visible = paw_visible | ~np.isnan(paw_vel_aligned)\n\n    # Per-session velocity thresholds (50th percentile on valid trials)\n    t_vals = tongue_vel_aligned[:, valid_trials][tongue_visible[:, valid_trials]]\n    t_vals = t_vals[~np.isnan(t_vals)]\n    tongue_thresh = np.percentile(t_vals, 50) if len(t_vals) > 0 else 0\n\n    p_vals = paw_vel_aligned[:, valid_trials][paw_visible[:, valid_trials]]\n    p_vals = p_vals[~np.isnan(p_vals)]\n    paw_thresh = np.percentile(p_vals, 50) if len(p_vals) > 0 else 0\n\n    me_thresh = 0\n    if me_available:\n        m_vals = me_aligned[:, valid_trials]\n        m_vals = m_vals[~np.isnan(m_vals)]\n        if len(m_vals) > 0:\n            me_thresh = np.percentile(m_vals, 50)\n\n    # Build per-trial data\n    neural_trials = []\n    input_trials = []\n    output_trials = []\n\n    for ti in valid_trials:\n        neural_trials.append(trialdat[:, :, ti].T.astype(np.float32))\n        input_trials.append(taxis.reshape(1, -1).astype(np.float32))\n\n        # Lick direction: 0=left, 1=right, 2=none\n        # R/L indicate cue direction. For hit trials, animal licked same direction.\n        # For miss trials, animal licked opposite direction. For ignore, no lick.\n        if sess['hit'][ti]:\n            lick_dir = 1 if sess['R'][ti] else 0\n        elif sess['miss'][ti]:\n            lick_dir = 0 if sess['R'][ti] else 1  # opposite of cue\n        else:\n            lick_dir = 2  # ignore/no response\n        # Context: 0=WC, 1=DR\n        context = 0 if sess['autowater'][ti] else 1\n        # Outcome: 0=incorrect, 1=correct, 2=ignore\n        outcome = 1 if sess['hit'][ti] else (0 if sess['miss'][ti] else 2)\n\n        # Tongue velocity discretized\n        tongue_disc = np.full(n_timebins, 2, dtype=np.int64)\n        vis = tongue_visible[:, ti] & ~np.isnan(tongue_vel_aligned[:, ti])\n        if vis.any():\n            tongue_disc[vis & (tongue_vel_aligned[:, ti] < tongue_thresh)] = 0\n            tongue_disc[vis & (tongue_vel_aligned[:, ti] >= tongue_thresh)] = 1\n\n        # Paw velocity discretized\n        paw_disc = np.full(n_timebins, 2, dtype=np.int64)\n        vis = paw_visible[:, ti] & ~np.isnan(paw_vel_aligned[:, ti])\n        if vis.any():\n            paw_disc[vis & (paw_vel_aligned[:, ti] < paw_thresh)] = 0\n            paw_disc[vis & (paw_vel_aligned[:, ti] >= paw_thresh)] = 1\n\n        # Motion energy discretized\n        me_disc = np.full(n_timebins, 2, dtype=np.int64)\n        if me_available:\n            valid_me = ~np.isnan(me_aligned[:, ti])\n            if valid_me.any():\n                me_disc[valid_me & (me_aligned[:, ti] < me_thresh)] = 0\n                me_disc[valid_me & (me_aligned[:, ti] >= me_thresh)] = 1\n\n        out = np.zeros((6, n_timebins), dtype=np.int64)\n        out[0, :] = lick_dir\n        out[1, :] = context\n        out[2, :] = outcome\n        out[3, :] = tongue_disc\n        out[4, :] = paw_disc\n        out[5, :] = me_disc\n        output_trials.append(out)\n\n    return {\n        'neural': neural_trials,\n        'input': input_trials,\n        'output': output_trials,\n        'anm': sess['anm'],\n        'date': sess['date'],\n        'n_units': n_units_final,\n    }\n\n\ndef main():\n    print(\"Loading and processing sessions...\")\n\n    all_sessions = []\n    all_subjects = []\n    subject_idx_list = []\n\n    for anm, date, probes, data_dir in SESSION_META:\n        print(f\"Loading {anm}_{date}...\")\n        try:\n            sess = load_session(anm, date, probes, data_dir)\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"  ERROR loading {anm}_{date}: {e}\")\n            continue\n\n        result = process_session(sess)\n        if result is None:\n            continue\n\n        if anm not in all_subjects:\n            all_subjects.append(anm)\n        subj_idx = all_subjects.index(anm)\n\n        all_sessions.append(result)\n        subject_idx_list.append(subj_idx)\n\n    print(f\"\\nTotal sessions: {len(all_sessions)}\")\n    print(f\"Total subjects: {len(all_subjects)}\")\n\n    neural = [s['neural'] for s in all_sessions]\n    inputs = [s['input'] for s in all_sessions]\n    outputs = [s['output'] for s in all_sessions]\n\n    brain_regions = ['ALM']\n    brain_region_idx = [np.zeros(s['n_units'], dtype=np.int64) for s in all_sessions]\n\n    data = {\n        'neural': neural,\n        'input': inputs,\n        'output': outputs,\n        'subjects': all_subjects,\n        'subject_idx': np.array(subject_idx_list, dtype=np.int64),\n        'brain_regions': brain_regions,\n        'brain_region_idx': brain_region_idx,\n        'input_names': ['time_from_go_cue'],\n        'output_names': [\n            'lick_direction', 'behavioral_context', 'outcome',\n            'tongue_velocity', 'paw_velocity', 'motion_energy',\n        ],\n        'output_values': [\n            ['left', 'right', 'none'],\n            ['WC', 'DR'],\n            ['incorrect', 'correct', 'ignore'],\n            ['below_50pct', 'above_50pct', 'not_visible'],\n            ['below_50pct', 'above_50pct', 'not_visible'],\n            ['below_50pct', 'above_50pct', 'no_video'],\n        ],\n        'metadata': {\n            'task_description': 'Two-context paradigm: delayed-response (DR) and water-cued (WC) directional licking tasks with ALM recordings',\n            'time_bin_size': DT * 1000,\n            'temporal_alignment_event': 'Go cue onset',\n            'off_start': TMIN,\n            'off_end': TMAX,\n 'min_units_per_session': MIN_UNITS,\n            'video_fps': VIDEO_FPS,\n            'session_info': [\n                {'subject': s['anm'], 'date': s['date'],\n                 'n_units':        pickle.dump(data, f)\n    print(f\"\\nSaved converted data to {out_path}\")\n\n    total_trials = sum(len(s['neural']) for s in all_sessions)\n    total_neurons = sum(s['n_units'] for s in Each session has `Ntrials` trials as defined by `bp.Ntrials`. Per-trial fields (`hit`, `miss`, `R`, `L`, `autowater`, `early`, `stim.enable`, `goCue`) are read as flat arrays of length `ntrials`. The agent used the Bpod trial structure directly, where each trial is defined by its index and associated metadata fields.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters are applied: early-lick trials (`bp.early`) and photostimulation trials (`bp.stim.enable`) are excluded. No filter is applied for trials that extend past the end of the neural recording.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_mask = ~sess['stim_enable'] & ~sess['early']\nvalid_trials = np.where(trial_mask)[0]\n```\n\niii. The agent followed the paper's convention of excluding early-lick and photostim trials. The agent did not implement a check for trials extending past the end of the recording.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. `obj.clu{probe}` spike-sorted clusters, specifically `clu.trial` (which trial each spike belongs to, 1-based) and `clu.trialtm` (spike time relative to trial start). Also `bp.ev.goCue` for alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\nspike_trials_raw = sess['spike_trials'][ui]\nspike_trialtm_raw = sess['spike_trialtm'][ui]\naligned_times = spike_trialtm_raw[spk_mask] - goCue[ti]\n```\n\niii. The agent identified `trial` and `trialtm` fields from the cluster data as the primary spike timing information, aligned to go cue.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are aligned to go cue, binned into 10 ms bins (DT = 1/100), converted to firing rates (Hz), then smoothed with a causal half-Gaussian kernel of window size 15 bins. The causal kernel is `gausswin(15)` with the first half zeroed out. Boundary condition is 'reflect' (prepend N reflected samples before convolution).\n\nii.\n```python\nDT = 1.0 / 100  # 10 ms bins\nSMOOTH_WINDOW = 15  # bins for causal Gaussian kernel\n\ndef causal_gaussian_kernel(N):\n    alpha = 2.5\n    n = np.arange(N)\n    w = np.exp(-0.5 * (alpha * (n - (N - 1) / 2) / ((N - 1) / 2)) ** 2)\n    w[:N // 2] = 0\n    w /= w.sum()\n    return w\n\ndef smooth_data(x, N, bc_type='reflect'):\n    kern = causal_gaussian_kernel(N)\n    if bc_type == 'reflect':\n        x_padded = np.concatenate([x[:N], x], axis=0)\n    ...\n    out[:, j] = np.convolve(x_padded[:, j], kern, mode='same')\n```\n\niii. The agent read `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` which specifies `params.dt = 1/100` and `params.smooth = 15`, and `mySmooth.m` which uses `gausswin(N)` with causal zeroing. The agent interpreted `params.dt = 1/100` as 10 ms bins.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters. First, clusters with quality labels in `{'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'}` are excluded (case-insensitive). Then units with mean firing rate ≤ 1 Hz are removed. Sessions with fewer than 10 remaining units are dropped entirely.\n\nii.\n```python\nEXCLUDED_QUALITIES = {'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'}\nLOW_FR_THRESH = 1.0\nMIN_UNITS = 10\n\nif q_str.lower() in {q.lower() for q in EXCLUDED_QUALITIES}:\n    continue\n...\nmean_frs = np.mean(trialdat[:, :, valid_trials], axis=(0, 2))\ngood_units = mean_frs > LOW_FR_THRESH\nif good_units.sum() < MIN_UNITS:\n    return None\n```\n\niii. Spike times carry trial indices (`clu.trial`) for assignment to trials.\n\nii.\n```python\nntrials = int(bp['Ntrials'][0, 0])\nhit = np.array(bp['hit']).flatten().astype(bool)\n...\nfor ti in range(ntrials):\n    spk_mask = spike_trials_raw == (ti + 1)\n```\n\niii. The agent used the Bpod trial structure directly, where each trial is defined by its index and associated metadata fields.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters are applied: early-lick trials (`bp.early`) and photostimulation trials (`bp.stim.enable`) are excluded. No filter is applied for trials that extend past the end of the neural recording.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_mask = ~sess['stim_enable'] & ~sess['early']\nvalid_trials = np.where(trial_mask)[0]\n```\n\niii. The agent followed the paper's convention of excluding early-lick and photostim trials. The agent did not implement a check for trials extending past the end of the recording.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. `obj.clu{probe}` spike-sorted clusters, specifically `clu.trial` (which trial each spike belongs to, 1-based) and `clu.trialtm` (spike time relative to trial start). Also `bp.ev.goCue` for alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\nspike_trials_raw = sess['spike_trials'][ui]\nspike_trialtm_raw = sess['spike_trialtm'][ui]\naligned_times = spike_trialtm_raw[spk_mask] - goCue[ti]\n```\n\niii. The agent identified `trial` and `trialtm` fields from the cluster data as the primary spike timing information, aligned to go cue.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are aligned to go cue, binned into 10 ms bins (DT = 1/100), converted to firing rates (Hz), then smoothed with a causal half-Gaussian kernel of window size 15 bins. The causal kernel is `gausswin(15)` with the first half zeroed out. Boundary condition is 'reflect' (prepend N reflected samples before convolution).\n\nii.\n```python\nDT = 1.0 / 100  # 10 ms bins\nSMOOTH_WINDOW = 15  # bins for causal Gaussian kernel\n\ndef causal_gaussian_kernel(N):\n    alpha = 2.5\n    n = np.arange(N)\n    w = np.exp(-0.5 * (alpha * (n - (N - 1) / 2) / ((N - 1) / 2)) ** 2)\n    w[:N // 2] = 0\n    w /= w.sum()\n    return w\n\ndef smooth_data(x, N, bc_type='reflect'):\n    kern = causal_gaussian_kernel(N)\n    if bc_type == 'reflect':\n        x_padded = np.concatenate([x[:N], x], axis=0)\n    ...\n    out[:, j] = np.convolve(x_padded[:, j], kern, mode='same')\n```\n\niii. The agent read `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` which specifies `params.dt = 1/100` and `params.smooth = 15`, and `mySmooth.m` which uses `gausswin(N)` with causal zeroing. The agent interpreted `params.dt = 1/100` as 10 ms bins.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters. First, clusters with quality labels in `{'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'}` are excluded (case-insensitive). Then units with mean firing rate ≤ 1 Hz are removed. Sessions with fewer than 10 remaining units are dropped entirely.\n\nii.\n```python\nEXCLUDED_QUALITIES = {'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'}\nLOW_FR_THRESH = 1.0\nMIN_UNITS = 10\n\nif q_str.lower() in {q.lower() for q in EXCLUDED_QUALITIES}:\n    continue\n...\nmean_frs = np.mean(trialdat[:, :, valid_trials], axis=(0, 2))\ngood_units = mean_frs > LOW_FR_THRESH\nif good_units.sum() < MIN_UNITS:\n    return None\n```\n\niii. The agent read `findClusters.m` which defines the quality filter as 'all' excluding `{'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'}`, and `removeLowFRClusters.m` for the 1 Hz threshold. The MIN_UNITS=10 filter was added by the agent as additional quality control.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Spike times (`trialtm`) are aligned to the go cue by subtracting `goCue[trial]` from each spike's trial-relative time. This produces spike times in seconds from go cue onset.\n\nii.\n```python\naligned_times = spike_trialtm_raw[spk_mask] - goCue[ti]\ncounts, _ = np.histogram(aligned_times, bins=edges)\n```\n\niii. The agent followed the reference `alignSpikes.m` which aligns to `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'`.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The time bin size is 10 ms (`DT = 1/100`), producing 500 bins across the -2.5 to 2.5 s window. No rebinning is applied; spikes are directly histogrammed into 10 ms bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nDT = 1.0 / 100  # 10 ms bins\nedges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT)\ntime_centers = edges[:-1] + DT / 2\n```\n\niii. The agent interpreted `params.dt = 1/100` from `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` as 10 ms (0.01 s) bins.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. This variable is constructed from the time bin centers, not from raw data. It represents the center of each time bin relative to go cue onset.\n\nii.\n```python\ntaxis = time_centers  # edges[:-1] + DT/2\ninput_trials.append(taxis.reshape(1, -1).astype(np.float32))\n```\n\niii. The time axis is defined by the binning grid parameters.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds*?\n\ni. No processing beyond computing bin centers from the bin edges.\n\nii.\n```python\nedges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT)\ntime_centers = edges[:-1] + DT / 2\n```\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. It is the same time axis used for neural binning. The same `edges` array defines both the spike histogram bins and the input time values.\n\nii.\n```python\ncounts, _ = np.histogram(aligned_times, bins=edges)\n...\ninput_trials.append(taxis.reshape(1, -1).astype(np.float32))\n```\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction* derived from?\n\ni. Three per-trial fields: `bp.hit`, `bp.miss`, and `bp.R` (right trial indicator). Lick direction is inferred from the combination of trial outcome and instructed direction.\n\nii.\n```python\nif sess['hit'][ti]:\n    lick_dir = 1 if sess['R'][ti] else 0\nelif sess['miss'][ti]:\n    lick_dir = 0 if sess['R'][ti] else 1  # opposite of cue\nelse:\n    lick_dir = 2  # ignore/no response\n```\n\niii. The agent initially missed the \"none\" category for ignore trials, then fixed it after noticing lick_direction only had values {0, 1}.\n\n## 5-a. Five steps: (1) Compute velocity as `sqrt(dx^2 + dy^2) * 400` using frame-to-frame differences at 400 Hz. (2) Frames with DLC confidence < 0.9 are marked not visible. (3) NaN values are NOT filled for tongue (per methods: \"except for the tongue\"). (4) Velocity is linearly interpolated from frame times to the 10 ms bin centers. (5) Split at per-session 50th percentile of visible values; non-visible bins get class 2.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_velocity(xy_coords, confidence=None, fill_missing=True):\n    dx = np.diff(xy[:, 0])\n    dy = np.diff(xy[:, 1])\n    vel = np.sqrt(dx**2 + dy**2) * VIDEO_FPS\n    vel = np.concatenate([[vel[0]], vel])\n    vel[~visible] = np.nan\n    return vel, visible\n\n# For tongue: fill_missing=False\nvel, vis = compute_velocity(tongue_xy, confidence=tongue_conf, fill_missing=False)\nf_interp = interp1d(ft_aligned, vel, kind='linear', bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\nv = f_interp(taxis)\n```\n\niii. The agent initially filled all missing values, then discovered this made all tongue values above threshold. After examining DLC confidence, the agent learned that tongue has mostly low confidence (~84% below 0.5) since it's only visible during licking, and corrected to not fill tongue missing values per the methods text.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session 50th percentile of visible (non-NaN, confidence >= 0.9) tongue velocity values. Below threshold → 0, at or above → 1, not visible → 2.\n\nii.\n```python\nt_vals = tongue_vel_aligned[:, valid_trials][tongue_visible[:, valid_trials]]\nt_vals = t_vals[~np.isnan(t_vals)]\ntongue_thresh = np.percentile(t_vals, 50) if len(t_vals) > 0 else 0\n\ntongue_disc = np.full(n_timebins, 2, dtype=np.int64)\nvis = tongue_visible[:, ti] & ~np.isnan(tongue_vel_aligned[:, ti])\ntongue_disc[vis & (tongue_vel_aligned[:, ti] < tongue_thresh)] = 0\ntongue_disc[vis & (tongue_vel_aligned[:, ti] >= tongue_thresh)] = 1\n```\n\niii. Follows the instruction's 50th percentile discretization scheme.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Per-session 50th percentile of visible paw velocity values. Several strategies: (1) DLC confidence < 0.9 marks frames as not visible for tongue and paw. (2) For tongue, NaN values are NOT filled (per methods). (3) For paw and motion energy, NaN values ARE filled with nearest-neighbor via `fill_nearest`. (4) Empty frame time arrays cause the trial's video data to remain NaN. (5) Trials with all-NaN video data get class 2 (\"not visible\"/\"no video\"). (6) Sessions loading errors are caught and skipped with a warning.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef fill_nearest(arr):\n    nans = np.isnan(arr)\n    valid = np.where(~nans)[0]\n    for idx in np.where(nans)[0]:\n        nearest = valid[np.argmin(np.abs(valid - idx))]\n        arr[idx] = arr[nearest]\n```\n\n```python\nvel[~visible] = np.nan\n```\n\niii. Early-lick trials (`bp.early`) and photostimulation trials (`bp.stim.enable`) are dropped, following the paper, which omits early licks from all analyses and treats photoinactivation as a separate experiment. Then trials that run past the end of the recording are dropped: in two sessions the behaviour continues after the probe stops, leaving trials with no spikes at all, and `haveEphys` does not flag them, so the cutoff is taken from the last trial in which any surviving unit fires. Early-lick and photostim removal follows the paper. The recording-length cut is needed because a trial after the probe stops would otherwise enter the dataset as 1000 bins of zero firing across every neuron.\n\n## 2-a. Counting every spike into a trial by bin grid in one call means spikes outside the window need no explicit handling, and the dropped trials are removed afterwards by selecting rows.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. The counts from 2-a are divided by the bin width to give spikes/s, then smoothed along time with a Gaussian of 14 ms standard deviation. Nothing else is done — no normalisation, no baseline subtraction, no z-scoring — so the stored values are firing rates in Hz. Units from both probes of a two-probe session are concatenated into one population.\n\nii.\n```python\nsigma = RATE_SD_MS / 1000 / BIN\nrates = []\nfor cluster in self.clusters:\n    rate = self.spike_count(cluster) / BIN                 # counts -> Hz\n    if rate.mean() > MIN_RATE:\n        rates.append(gaussian_filter1d(rate, sigma, axis=1, mode='reflect'))\n```\n\niii. 14 ms is the standard deviation of the reference's smoothing kernel: `params.smooth = 15` builds a `gausswin(15)`, whose sigma is `(15-1)/(2 × 2.5) = 2.8` samples, and at 5 ms bins that is 14 ms.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters.
